- **Decision**: **(product call), 2026-07-28** — (1) **no cancellation credits** at launch (AC #86 dormant; `end_of_term` default; operator handles exceptions); (2) **cross-boundary cancel+new signed off** + GTM constraint entry required; (3) **seats×usage single-line stays Future** (two-row workaround; the seat-scaled-allowance boundary = the named D-45 gate); (4) **no freemium structural flag** (PlanTier `free` / plan-shape derivation; additive reopen if Analytics fails); (5) **enforced-migration notice = configurable, 60-day default floor**, validated at scheduling. **Propagated**: PRD §15 rows ×5 (cancellation credits / cross-boundary sign-off / seats×usage / freemium flag / migration notice — each row cites D-49), PRD `fr-scheduled-migration` (the notice floor), S11 M5 + `inst-mg-target` (citation made resolver-legible 2026-07-31 — the sites already carried D-49).

#### D-50 [H] PriceOverlay mutations are always material

- **Where**: [`design/05-governance.md`](./design/05-governance.md) `inst-mat-registered` + DoD; [`design/09-price-overlays.md`](./design/09-price-overlays.md) authoring flow RETURN step; PRD approval-threshold FR.
- **Problem** (2026-07-28 design review, blocking #1): the `MaterialityEvaluator` computes per-row deltas over **price rows**; an overlay is not a price row, so with a threshold policy configured an overlay edit produced no delta → `auto_publishable`. Editing a `global`/`partner` line from −10% to −90% reached consumers with **no approver** (`price_overlay × write` only), while a $1 price-row change above threshold required two people.
- **Decision**: **(product call), 2026-07-28** — overlay creation, line add/remove, any line magnitude/kind change, and audience changes (scope/precedence/dating/disclosure) are **always-material registered triggers**: percent lines carry no currency and lines have no per-currency baseline, so the G1 no-delta fail-safe applies wholesale; the commit routes through the approval workflow before its D-06 publish unit fires. **Propagated**: S5 `inst-mat-registered` + materiality DoD; S9 flow; PRD FR.

#### D-51 [H] Retirement preserves in-flight coverage

- **Where**: [`design/11-lifecycle.md`](./design/11-lifecycle.md) `inst-rt-cancel` + `inst-re-cancelflow`; [`design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md`](./design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md) `inst-co-retirement-unwind` note.
- **Problem** (2026-07-28 design review, blocking #2): retirement cancelled **every** not-yet-active window; a scheduled supersession successor `W2 = [t2, null)` was cancelled while active `W1 = [t0, t2)` ran to its natural end — every in-flight subscriber lost rateability from `t2` (arrears + renewals fail closed per `inst-ws-expire`; renewals are never gate-blocked, so they kept coming). `inst-fg-detect` only sees gaps *between* windows; D-05 had fixed exactly this trailing void, but only for live cutover units.
- **Decision**: **(product call), 2026-07-28** — retirement cancels scheduled windows **only for keys with no in-flight subscribers**; a key's continuing-coverage window is kept. Retirement stops **selling** (sellability-gate lifecycle predicate), never **rating**; the confirm screen labels kept vs cancelled windows. D-05's unwind composes (it restores predecessor coverage). **Propagated**: S11 `inst-rt-cancel`/`inst-re-cancelflow`; S7 unwind note.

#### D-52 [M] Grants are a table (`pricing_plan_grant`)

- **Where**: [`design/10-advanced-primitives.md`](./design/10-advanced-primitives.md) §6 + touches lists.
- **Problem** (2026-07-28 design review #3): `inst-ac-carry` compiles a promotional grant **per allowance-carrying usage row**, but the schema held one `pricing_plan.prepaid_grant` jsonb and `pricing_grant_price` keyed by `plan_id` — a plan with two carry rows, or an authored grant plus one carry row, had nowhere to put the second grant.
- **Decision**: **2026-07-28** — `pricing_plan_grant` (PK `grant_id`, `source ∈ {authored, compiled_allowance}` with source `price_id` lineage); `pricing_grant_price` re-keyed on `grant_id`; D-43 field rules apply per grant row; compiled grants always `promotional`, never priced, recompiled never hand-edited. **Propagated**: S10 §6 schema + touches.

#### D-53 [M] Reservation on level rows: capacity flavor only at launch

- **Where**: [`design/10-advanced-primitives.md`](./design/10-advanced-primitives.md) `inst-rv-level` + §5 code; [`design/03-price-structure.md`](./design/03-price-structure.md) `inst-la-composite` cross-ref.
- **Problem** (2026-07-28 design review #11): `inst-rv-tier-q` subtracts the matched reserved quantity from `Q`; under D-44 a non-`sum` `Q` is the sum of per-granule level folds, where "subtract" has no single meaning (per granule vs per window) — and reserved cloudlets metered peak-per-hour is a likely launch product.
- **Decision**: **(product call), 2026-07-28** — on non-`sum` rows only `reservationFlavor = capacity` is authorable (`capacityCharge = reservedRate × reservedQuantity` never touches `Q` — exactly the reserved-cloudlets shape); `consumption` fails publish (`LEVEL_RESERVATION_CONSUMPTION_FORBIDDEN`, 422) until per-granule netting semantics are decided (named Future gate); the reservation fixture gains a capacity-on-level scenario. **Propagated**: S10 `inst-rv-level` + §5; S3 cross-ref. *The two-factor formula gloss above is **superseded by D-139** (2026-08-02): `reservedRate` is money per granule, so the charge carries a covered-duration factor. The decision itself — capacity-only on non-`sum` rows, `consumption` publish-blocked — is unchanged.*

#### D-54 [M] `comparabilityRank` reverse guard

- **Where**: [`design/06-consumer-contracts.md`](./design/06-consumer-contracts.md) `inst-pc-mutual` + §5 code.
- **Problem** (2026-07-28 design review #12): rank presence was validated only from the **source** plan's publish; a target offering no outbound changes could re-publish with `comparability_rank` NULL, leaving published inbound edges unclassifiable at read time — the same drift class D-23 exists to prevent (D-24 covers only retirement).
- **Decision**: **2026-07-28** — a plan referenced by any published `allowedChangeTargets` edge MUST NOT re-publish with its rank dropped (`COMPARABILITY_RANK_REVOKED`, 422, referencing plans enumerated); dropping the rank requires removing the inbound edges first or publishing a covering `PlanTier` ordering. **Propagated**: S6 `inst-pc-mutual` + §5.

#### D-55 [M] Rev-share requires `sum_of_parts` at launch

- **Where**: [`design/08-bundles.md`](./design/08-bundles.md) `inst-rs-sum` + key constraints; PRD `fr-bundle-composition` + §17.3 row.
- **Problem** (2026-07-28 design review #13): rev-share sums per vendor SKU, but an `own_price` bundle has one bundle amount and no per-vendor-SKU revenue — no declared allocation base; Marketplace cannot accrue per SKU. The PRD claimed "rev-share and itemization are independent of the basis".
- **Decision**: **(product call), 2026-07-28** — rev-share is authorable on `sum_of_parts` bundles only; `own_price` + revShare fails publish (`REVSHARE_BASIS_UNSUPPORTED`, 422); lifting requires deciding an allocation base (e.g. component list prices) — a named Future gate. Bundles are p2, so this is a stated launch limitation, not a model change. Same review also fixed the residual-absorber typing: absorption is **per `(bundle, vendor SKU)` group** with a **party-typed** `residual_absorber_party` on the new `pricing_bundle_revshare_group` row (the bundle-level vendor-SKU absorber named a group, not a party), and `platform_cut_bp` moved to the group row. **Propagated**: S8 schema/algo/constraints; PRD FR + §17.3 + AC #23.

#### D-56 [H] Plan revision representation — draft-revision rows

- **Where**: [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §3.7 `pricing_plan` row + §4.3 `VersioningStore`; [`design/02-plan-definition.md`](./design/02-plan-definition.md) §6 `pricing_plan_phase.plan_revision` + §9 integration AC.
- **Problem** (2026-07-28 code-readiness review, blocking #1): Foundation said `pricing_plan` is "updated in place only through DraftStateMachine transitions" while S2's integration AC said "a published plan's shape change creates a new revision (append-only), never mutates in place" — and with a single mutable row, one `lifecycle_state`, and PATCH legal only in `draft`, a draft of revision N+1 had no defined place to live while revision N stayed published. The Foundation is the normative owner of the versioning mechanic and never specified the storage/authoring shape; `pricing_plan_phase.plan_revision` semantics were likewise undefined and, naively re-minting phase rows per revision, would have changed continuing rows' id-typed `phase` axis (D-19) and broken same-key supersession.
- **Decision**: **(review fix), 2026-07-28 — flagged for veto** — **draft-revision rows**: `pricing_plan` is keyed **`(plan_id, revision)`**; published revision rows are **immutable**; a shape change on a published plan opens a **new revision row in `draft`** (at most one open draft per plan — partial unique on `lifecycle_state = 'draft'`); the plan's identity, scope-key axes, and `pricing_price` attachment stay on `plan_id` (unchanged). Phase rows **re-attach** to the new revision with **stable `phase_id`** (never re-minted; `plan_revision` on the phase row = the revision that last (re-)attached it), preserving the `phase` axis and same-key supersession. In-place mutation survives only as state-machine `lifecycle_state` flips on a revision row — supersedes R-12's "`pricing_plan` mutable-in-place" clause to that extent. **Propagated**: Foundation §3.7 + §4.3; S2 phase-table note + stable-`phase_id` rule + integration AC.

#### D-57 [H] PRD realignment — `flat` is a non-usage kind, the untiered metered rate is `per_unit`

- **Where**: [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-model-kind`, §17.1 kind table (`Flat`, `Per-unit`, `Usage-based` rows), §17.4 (per-unit quantity source + evaluation-policy placement), AC #14, AC #15.
- **Problem** (2026-07-29 review, core-model pass): the 2026-07-28 fix rerouted the plain untiered metered rate from `flat` to `per_unit` **in S3 only**. Every PRD counterpart kept the pre-fix shape: §17.4 required `quantitySource` on *every* `per_unit` row and forbade `billingGranularity` on *every* `per_unit` row; §17.1 defined `per_unit` as "**Recurring** unit price × external quantity"; AC #15 ("Flat usage unit price") explicitly blessed `modelKind=flat` on a usage row, which `inst-mk-chargekind` now rejects with `MODEL_KIND_CHARGEKIND_MISMATCH`; and `fr-model-kind` banned tiered kinds on non-usage rows but never banned `flat` on usage. The single most common usage row in the launch set (`$0.10/GB` egress) was therefore unpublishable under one document or the other, whichever the implementer read.
- **Decision**: **(review fix), 2026-07-29** — the **slice wins**; the D-18 kind × chargeKind matrix is stated as total in `fr-model-kind` and mirrored everywhere: `flat`/`per_unit` on non-usage rows, `per_unit`/`graduated`/`volume`/`package` on usage rows, `flat`-on-usage fails publish. `quantitySource` is REQUIRED on `per_unit` **non-usage** rows and forbidden on `per_unit` **usage** rows (the meter supplies `Q`); `billingGranularity` is carried by every usage row including untiered ones. AC #15 is renamed "Untiered usage unit price" and re-authored against `per_unit`, with the `flat`-on-usage rejection as an explicit clause. **Propagated**: PRD `fr-model-kind`, §17.1 ×3 rows, §17.4 ×2 rows, AC #14, AC #15.

#### D-58 [H] `package` rows carry `tierAggregationWindow`

- **Where**: [`design/03-price-structure.md`](./design/03-price-structure.md) `inst-pk-fields`/`inst-pk-window`/`inst-pk-math`/`inst-tb-window` + `tier_aggregation_window` column; [PRD](./PRD.md) §17.1 usage row, §17.7 reservation evaluation-policy row.
- **Problem**: `package` is a usage kind whose math (`blocks = ceil(used / packageSize)`) is **non-linear in the accumulation window**, but `tierAggregationWindow` was required only on *tiered* rows and the schema said "tiered usage rows only" — so a published `package` row named no window at all. `volume`, equally non-linear, has its window stated explicitly in §17.2 ("single rate on total `Q` **within `tierAggregationWindow`**"); `package` had no counterpart. 150 units in a month folds to 2 blocks under `invoice_period` and 30 under a daily reading — a 15× spread on a row that passes every stated check, with the golden fixture arbitrating only in favour of whoever wrote it first.
- **Options**: (a) require `tierAggregationWindow` on `package` rows; (b) declare an implicit default (invoice period); (c) leave it to Tariffs.
- **Decision**: **(a), 2026-07-29 — flagged for veto** (it makes a previously optional field mandatory on an existing kind, so an authoring surface changes). (b) rejected — "no implicit default at rating time" is the standing rule for every other evaluation-policy field; (c) rejected — it is an authored commercial choice, not a formula. Publish fails `EVAL_POLICY_MISSING` when absent. **Propagated**: S3 `inst-pk-window` (new) + `inst-pk-math` + `inst-tb-window` + schema column note; PRD §17.1 usage row + §17.7 reservation row.

#### D-59 [H] `includedAllowance`: forbidden on `volume`, kind-rewriting on untiered rows

- **Where**: [`design/10-advanced-primitives.md`](./design/10-advanced-primitives.md) `inst-ac-gate` + `inst-ac-band` + §5 codes + DoD; [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-included-allowance`, AC #90.
- **Problem** (two defects in one primitive): (1) the gate's fail set excluded only `package`, so `includedAllowance` was authorable on **`volume`** — but catalog `volume` is Tariffs **Variant A** (single rate on the **total** `Q`, Q3), under which the compiled `$0` first band is a **cliff**, not an allowance: with `N = 100` and a `$1` band, `Q = 99` bills `$0` and `Q = 101` bills **`$101`**, while `inst-ac-marker` displays "includes 100 units". (2) On an untiered usage row the compiler synthesizes a two-band graduated set, but S3 forbids band rows on `per_unit` at the validator **and** at the DB (composite FK / trigger on `model_kind IN ('graduated','volume')`), and never authorized the compiler to rewrite the kind — so every untiered allowance died on a DB trigger inside the publish transaction rather than producing a validation report.
- **Decision**: **(review fix), 2026-07-29 — flagged for veto** (it narrows the authorable surface). (1) `volume` joins `package` in `ALLOWANCE_KIND_UNSUPPORTED`: an allowance under Variant A would have to bill `(Q − N) × rate`, which the band model cannot express, so it is refused at publish rather than approximated. `includedAllowance` is authorable on `graduated` rows and untiered `per_unit` usage rows only. (2) The compile **rewrites** `model_kind` to `graduated` in the publish transaction — the authored `amount_minor` moves into the `[N, null)` band's `unit_price_minor` and `amount_minor` goes NULL (satisfying `AMOUNT_PLACEMENT_INVALID`); the row is fixture-gated and snapshot-frozen as `graduated`, with the authored kind retained beside the marker. **Propagated**: S10 `inst-ac-gate`, `inst-ac-band`, §5 codes, DoD (+1 new case); PRD `fr-included-allowance`, AC #90.

#### D-60 [H] Trailing-tier lock is a Rating-owned per-period pin, and `trailing_period` is fixture-gated

- **Where**: [`design/10-advanced-primitives.md`](./design/10-advanced-primitives.md) `inst-tt-lock`/`inst-tt-bootstrap`/`inst-tt-window-pair`/`inst-tt-zero-band`/`inst-tt-fixture` + §5 codes; [`design/03-price-structure.md`](./design/03-price-structure.md) `FixtureGate` row; [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-trailing-tier-qualification`; rating [`SEAMS.md`](../../rating/docs/SEAMS.md) M12.
- **Problem** (three defects on one D-40 primitive, found independently by the core-model and cross-doc passes): (1) the qualified rate was "frozen into `pricingSnapshotRef`" — but that reference is plan-scoped, stamped once at publish and immutable (Foundation §4.4), while the lock is per-subscription per-billing-period; one published row serves N subscriptions with N anchors and N trailing totals, so the locked rate had nowhere durable to live and replay-determinism — the stated reason for freezing — was unachievable. (2) The trailing total ("the prior **billing** period's total `Q`") was never constrained against `tierAggregationWindow`, which keys the in-period counter to a different window; and nothing forbade a `$0` lowest band, under which bootstrap makes the whole first period free at any volume and an alternating usage pattern bills nothing forever. (3) Rating SEAMS M12 asserts "**blocks any `trailing_period` row publish until adopted**", but publish gating lives in pricing and pricing had **no** gate, no code and no DoD clause — D-44 got a hard `FIXTURE_MISSING` gate for the identical joint dependency, D-40 got "MAY set".
- **Decision**: **(review fix), 2026-07-29 — flagged for veto** — (1) the catalog freezes **only** the authored window and band set; **Rating** pins `{priceId, periodStart, qualifiedBandId, lockedUnitPriceMinor}` on the period's rated charges beside `pricingSnapshotRef`, and that pin is the replay source. This is what rating's own M12 already called the "locked-rate pin read by step 2", so both sides now describe one mechanism. (2) The trailing total is a separate aggregate over the prior anchor-derived period, independent of `tierAggregationWindow`; `trailing_period` with `tierAggregationWindow ∈ {subscription_lifetime, per_event}` fails publish (`TIER_QUAL_WINDOW_INCOMPATIBLE`), as does a `$0` lowest band (`TIER_QUAL_ZERO_BAND_LOCK`). (3) `trailing_tier` is a registered `FixtureGate` variant; publish without the green joint fixture is blocked (`FIXTURE_MISSING`), making M12's asserted block real. **Propagated**: S10 ×5 instructions + §5 codes; S3 `FixtureGate` row; PRD `fr-trailing-tier-qualification`; rating SEAMS M12 (also corrected there: the superseded "or an authored bootstrap" clause).

#### D-61 [H] Reviewability invariant — the approver must hold `read` on what they approve

- **Where**: [`design/05-governance.md`](./design/05-governance.md) AuthZ resource catalog (`historical_import` actions), endpoint map, role matrix, and the new normative note under it.
- **Problem**: D-50 made every `PriceOverlay` edit always-material and D-13 made every historical import always-material — but the role matrix gave `FinanceReviewer` only `approval × approve/read`, `approval_policy × write/read`, `plan × read`. No `price_overlay × read` (which the non-approving `FinanceManager` *does* hold), no `customer_group × read`, no `bundle × read`, and `historical_import` defined no `read` action at all. Deny-by-default then denied the approver every surface that would show them the subject, while `fr-approval-two-person` requires "a reviewer can only approve exactly what they reviewed" — so the two-person rule was formally satisfied and substantively hash-blind.
- **Decision**: **(review fix), 2026-07-29** — `FinanceReviewer` gains `bundle × read`, `price_overlay × read`, `customer_group × read`, `historical_import × read`; a `read` action and a `GET /bss-pricing/v1/historical-imports/{id}` surface are added. Stated as a **standing invariant**, not a one-off patch: for every registered always-material trigger the approving role MUST hold `read` on the trigger's subject, so adding a trigger to `inst-mat-registered` also requires the matching grant — and `GET /bss-pricing/v1/approvals/{id}` MUST return the **pinned content** the `content_hash` covers, never the hash alone. **Propagated**: S5 resource catalog + endpoint map + role matrix + notes block.

#### D-62 [H] Window cancellation and `effectiveTo` shortening are always-material and trailing-void-checked

- **Where**: [`design/05-governance.md`](./design/05-governance.md) `inst-mat-registered`; [`design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md`](./design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md) `inst-fg-trailing` (new) + §5 codes.
- **Problem**: D-05 and D-51 both diagnose the trailing void and both guard **only** the retirement path. `DELETE /bss-pricing/v1/price-windows/{id}` and the shortening `PATCH` carry the identical hazard under plain `plan × write`, were absent from the always-material trigger list, and are invisible to `inst-fg-detect` — an *interior* check that compares each window to its successor and by construction cannot see a void with no successor. So one operator could delete the two-person-approved scheduled successor, let the active window expire at its natural end, and leave every arrears charge and renewal on the key failing closed — while S7 claims "there is **no side door** … a gap can never be introduced past validation".
- **Decision**: **(review fix), 2026-07-29** — both operations join the always-material triggers, and a **trailing-void predicate** is added: a cancel/shorten MUST leave the key covered through `max(current coverage end, now + the longest billing cycle sold on that key)` or reject (`WINDOW_TRAILING_VOID`, 422). The D-51 exemption (no in-flight subscribers) applies to both the materiality trigger and the gap check. **Propagated**: S5 `inst-mat-registered`; S7 `inst-fg-trailing` + §5 codes. **Amended by D-80 (2026-07-30)**: the exemption additionally requires the key **not currently sellable** (it raced the gate for future subscribers), and its predicate's data source is D-79.

#### D-63 [M] `effectiveFrom` MUST be strictly future at window creation

- **Where**: [`design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md`](./design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md) `inst-ws-future-start` (new) + §5 codes.
- **Problem**: the doc set bans a past `availableFrom` on every billing cycle and requires the cutover instant to be future at submit *and* commit — but said nothing about `effectiveFrom` at window **creation**, and the window (not `availableFrom`) is what Tariffs step 2 resolves against. `inst-ws-immutable` guards only *mutation* of a past start. A `plan × write` holder could POST a window starting 60 days ago, have the activation job flip it on its next pass, and reprice open arrears periods — with no `BackdateGrant`, no reason, no second person, and without ever evaluating `BACKDATE_SIDE_EFFECT`, the predicate written precisely for "an effective range intersecting a not-yet-closed billing period".
- **Decision**: **(review fix), 2026-07-29** — `effective_from` MUST be strictly in the future at creation (`WINDOW_START_IN_PAST`, 422). Retroactive effectiveness exists only as reference rows on the S5 historical-import path, which schedules no windows. **Propagated**: S7 `inst-ws-future-start` + §5 codes.

#### D-64 [M] The terminal `phase_id` is immutable across plan revisions

- **Where**: [`design/02-plan-definition.md`](./design/02-plan-definition.md) `inst-ph-terminal-stable` (new) + §5 codes.
- **Problem**: D-56 pins phase **ids**, but both the scope-key `phase` default and D-15 usage phase-invariance are defined relative to *which* phase is **terminal** — and nothing stopped a revision from re-terminalizing (add an evergreen phase, give the old terminal a `convertsToPhaseId`) or from dropping a phase still referenced by published rows. `PHASE_UNCOVERED` checks only the converse direction, and since the 2026-07-28 fix it is scoped to recurring rows on `recurring`/`hybrid` plans — so usage-only plans are entirely unguarded. A usage-only plan whose metered row sits on the implicit terminal `T0` can be revised into a phased plan where `T0` is no longer terminal: a subscription in the new evergreen phase then resolves **no** usage row and Tariffs fails closed on a published, sellable plan — the "sold but unrateable" state D-15 exists to prevent, reintroduced through revisioning.
- **Decision**: **(review fix), 2026-07-29** — (a) a revision MUST NOT re-terminalize an existing phase or introduce a different terminal phase (`TERMINAL_PHASE_CHANGED`, 422); (b) a revision MUST re-attach every `phase_id` referenced by a current published price row (`PHASE_IN_USE`, 422). **Propagated**: S2 `inst-ph-terminal-stable` + §5 codes.

#### D-65 [H] Migration execution handshake — `/start` / `/complete`

- **Where**: [`design/11-lifecycle.md`](./design/11-lifecycle.md) §5 + `inst-mst-start`/`inst-mst-complete`; [PRD](./PRD.md) §9.2 `contract-subscriptions`; [`design/05-governance.md`](./design/05-governance.md) endpoint map; subscriptions [`SEAMS.md`](../../subscriptions/docs/SEAMS.md) **SUB-P7** (new).
- **Problem** (reached independently from both sides of the seam): pricing's migration state machine names Subscriptions as the actor of **both** non-terminal transitions — `scheduled → in_progress` "when the effective date passes **and Subscriptions begins `PlanLink` execution**", `in_progress → completed` "when Subscriptions reports the scope processed" — while the API surface exposed only operator-facing `POST`/`DELETE`/`GET`, and pricing §9.2 declared exactly one inbound lane (grandfathering re-bind). On the Subscriptions side the register carried **zero** migration rows, though every other pricing seam is registered SUB-P1…SUB-P6. D-34 (in-flight cancel), D-36 (execution-time lock + boundary re-validation) and D-38 (T-ε race) all rest on this handshake: without it the catalog flips on wall clock alone, D-36 never runs, and the freshly re-resolved exclusion set has no delivery path — a subscription contract-locked between scheduling and `effective_at` gets migrated, breaking `dod-contract-lock`'s "reported, never broken", and the record stays `scheduled` forever so `pricing.migration.stalled` cannot tell stalled from finished.
- **Decision**: **(review fix), 2026-07-29 — joint** — two service-identity endpoints: `POST /bss-pricing/v1/migrations/{id}/start` (flips `scheduled → in_progress`, runs the D-36 re-validation, **returns the exclusion set** Subscriptions MUST honour; idempotent on `migration_id`) and `POST /bss-pricing/v1/migrations/{id}/complete` (processed / excluded / failed sets). Calling `/start` before the first `PlanLink` batch is a normative obligation, alongside the existing per-batch state re-read. **Propagated**: S11 §5 + both transitions; PRD §9.2 (now two inbound lanes); S5 endpoint map (`plan × migrate` for the Subscriptions service identity); subscriptions SEAMS **SUB-P7**. **Owed**: the Subscriptions-side executor (start call, per-batch re-read, dedup key, completion report) before design freeze — pricing's D-36/D-38 text is normative. **Sharpened 2026-07-31 (PR-review follow-up):** `/start` idempotency is **persist-and-replay** — the exclusion set is computed once at the first `/start`, persisted keyed by `migration_id`, and every retry returns **that stored snapshot verbatim**, never a recomputation (a recompute could differ from the set the executor already honoured mid-run, splitting one run across two exclusion sets); the D-36 re-validation runs exactly once, at the first call.

#### D-66 [M] Cross-gear seam drift — four corrections

- **Where**: rating [`design/11-consumer-contracts.md`](../../rating/docs/design/11-consumer-contracts.md), [`design/01-foundation.md`](../../rating/docs/design/01-foundation.md), [`DESIGN.md`](../../rating/docs/DESIGN.md), [`design/04-overlays-precedence.md`](../../rating/docs/design/04-overlays-precedence.md); subscriptions [`PRD.md`](../../subscriptions/docs/PRD.md), [`design/05-entitlements.md`](../../subscriptions/docs/design/05-entitlements.md).
- **Problem**: four places where a consumer gear's view of the pricing contract disagreed with pricing. (1) **`CatalogVersionPublished` emitter** — rating attributed it to pricing's outbox, ordered per `(tenant, plan)`, in five places; it is not in pricing's frozen event-name set and the **registry (products gear)** is the sole incrementer, emitting tenant-wide. Wiring the pin adapter to pricing's topic means no `CatalogVersion` ever becomes pin-eligible and every resolution run fails closed at integration. (2) **`coverage_incomplete`** — pricing designs an uncovered currency as an expected drift state resolving at base price (D-08 remediation path, Warn alarm); rating called the same runtime observation "a defensive fail-closed", which would make every subscription in a newly launched currency unrateable behind a Warn. (3) **Promotional trial windows** — subscriptions still named "promotional `PriceWindow`" as an authoritative trial-offer form in two places, though pricing defines no such window kind (ADR-0003) and trials are phases; SUB-P3 already recorded the removal as done. (4) **Per-seat quotas** — subscriptions materialises `grant × committed quantity` "where the pricing grant set marks a quota per-seat", but pricing publishes exactly two entry shapes and carries no such marker; seat scaling is an explicitly named pricing Future gate (D-49(3)).
- **Decision**: **(review fix), 2026-07-29** — **pricing wins all four**; the consumer-side text is corrected. (1) two producers, two streams, stated normatively with the ordering caveat. (2) rating adopts pricing's drop-that-overlay-and-continue rule. (3) the third enumeration member is struck, completing SUB-P3. (4) the per-seat branch is deferred behind pricing's Future gate with the explicit reading `quotaKey: 20` = 20 **total**, never 20/seat. **Propagated**: cross-gear only — `../../rating/docs/design/11-consumer-contracts.md`, `../../rating/docs/design/01-foundation.md`, `../../rating/docs/DESIGN.md`, `../../rating/docs/design/04-overlays-precedence.md` (6 sites); `../../subscriptions/docs/PRD.md`, `../../subscriptions/docs/design/05-entitlements.md` (3 sites) — no pricing-side document changed.

#### D-67 [M] Overlay magnitude bounds — landed; the stacked-result floor stays OPEN

- **Where**: [`design/09-price-overlays.md`](./design/09-price-overlays.md) `adjustment_value` column + §5 codes.
- **Problem**: no rule capped `adjustment_value`, no rule capped an amount-based `discount` against the base, and no rule floored the resolved price at zero. The `≥ 0` money constraint binds authored **price rows**, not overlay lines, and the publish-blocking condition lists enumerate duplicate line keys, out-of-scope targets, missing per-currency values and undeclared tax basis — never magnitude. `discount / percent_bp = 15000` (the "150% of list" data-entry inversion) published cleanly, and with D-61's gap the approver could not even see it.
- **Decision**: **(review fix), 2026-07-29, partial** — the unambiguous half lands: `discount` bp ∈ `(0, 10000]`, `markup` bp > 0, amount magnitudes ≥ 0 at the currency's minor unit, else `ADJUSTMENT_MAGNITUDE_OUT_OF_RANGE` (422, naming the line). The **stacked-result floor** is deliberately **not** decided here — see §F.1: clamping at 0 versus rejecting at publish is a commercial call, and it belongs in the same joint contract as `fixed` semantics and stack direction. **Propagated**: S9 schema column + §5 codes.

#### D-68 [L] Register-propagation repairs

- **Where**: [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-reserved-capacity` + §17.7 ×2 + `fr-plan-change-contract` + §1.4 glossary + §17.6 + AC #108 + §17.8 (new row) + `fr-trailing-tier-qualification`; [`design/03-price-structure.md`](./design/03-price-structure.md) + [`design/10-advanced-primitives.md`](./design/10-advanced-primitives.md) Traces-to.
- **Problem** (cross-doc pass, Job 1): the register's hand-written "propagation surface" field is a promise nobody verifies. **D-53** listed only S10 + S3 and never reached the PRD — `fr-reserved-capacity` and §17.7 still permitted `consumption` on level rows unconditionally, including the tier-`Q` exclusion D-53 says has no meaning against a granule fold, and `D-53` appeared **zero** times in the PRD. The S6 K4 fix cutting the **`PlanTier`-ordering escape hatch** was unregistered and survived in four PRD places, pointing at a §17.8 row that did not exist. D-40's 2026-07-28 amendment recorded the bootstrap change but not the **`TIER_QUAL_ON_NON_TIERED` widening** (explicit `current` now also rejected), leaving PRD and slice validators disagreeing. Two FR ids (`fr-level-aggregation`, `fr-trailing-tier-qualification`) — the only 2 of 66 — were claimed by no slice's **Traces-to**, though both behaviours are designed. **D-52** landed design-side only, leaving §17.7's grant table describing a single plan-attached grant with `expiryPolicy ∈ {never, days(N)}`.
- **Decision**: **(review fix), 2026-07-29** — all repaired against the design side, and the §17.8 Future row the K4 fix pointed at is created. **Standing consequence** (see §F.2): a decision's propagation surface is not closed until the PRD row, the enforcing validator/grant, and the consumer-side mirror are each named and checked — the "record the rule, skip the enforcement point" pattern produced 13 of this round's 31 findings.
- **Propagated**: PRD `fr-reserved-capacity` + §17.7 + `fr-plan-change-contract` + §1.4 + §17.6 + AC #108 + §17.8 + `fr-trailing-tier-qualification`; [`design/03-price-structure.md`](./design/03-price-structure.md) Traces-to; [`design/10-advanced-primitives.md`](./design/10-advanced-primitives.md) Traces-to. *(Field added 2026-07-31 — the entry carried its surface only in prose **Where**, invisible to the P1 resolver; this was the register's one remaining entry with no machine-readable propagation claim.)*

#### D-69 [H] D-60 propagation completed — glossary, Scope table, and the slice's own DoD

- **Where**: [PRD](./PRD.md) §1.4 glossary (Trailing-tier qualification), §5.1 In Scope row; [`design/10-advanced-primitives.md`](./design/10-advanced-primitives.md) `dod-trailing-tier`.
- **Problem** (2026-07-30 propagation pass): D-60 replaced "the qualified rate is frozen into `pricingSnapshotRef`" with the Rating-owned per-period pin, and recorded its propagation surface as "S10 ×5 instructions + §5 codes; S3 `FixtureGate` row; PRD `fr-trailing-tier-qualification`". Three sites carrying the **superseded** wording were not in that list and survived: the PRD glossary entry, the PRD §5.1 Scope note, and — most consequentially — `dod-trailing-tier` in D-60's own slice, which still read "locks that rate for the current period **into `pricingSnapshotRef`**". So the PRD contradicted itself between §1.4 and §6.10, and the clause that gates "done" for the primitive described exactly the model D-60 proved impossible (plan-scoped, publish-time, immutable vs. per-subscription per-period). The DoD also named none of D-60's three new publish blocks — `TIER_QUAL_WINDOW_INCOMPATIBLE`, `TIER_QUAL_ZERO_BAND_LOCK`, `FIXTURE_MISSING` — although D-60 was closed precisely on the complaint that the fixture gate had "no gate, no code and **no DoD clause** in pricing" (D-59, decided the same day, did update its DoD).
- **Decision**: **(review fix), 2026-07-30 — flagged for veto** (it inherits D-60's own veto flag; vetoing D-60 must revert these three sites with it). All three restated against `inst-tt-lock`: the catalog freezes only the authored window and band set; Rating pins `{priceId, periodStart, qualifiedBandId, lockedUnitPriceMinor}` beside the snapshot and that pin is the replay source. `dod-trailing-tier` additionally enumerates the three publish blocks and the bootstrap pin. **Propagated**: PRD §1.4 + §5.1 + `fr-trailing-tier-qualification` (already correct, unchanged); S10 `dod-trailing-tier`.

#### D-70 [M] D-58 propagation completed — the `package` window becomes a requirement anyone can test

- **Where**: [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-package-pricing`, `fr-rating-compatibility`, §17.4 evaluation-policy-placement row, AC #13, AC #88, AC #89, AC #26; [`design/03-price-structure.md`](./design/03-price-structure.md) `dod-package` + `dod-tier-bands`.
- **Problem**: D-58 made `tierAggregationWindow` mandatory on `package` rows and propagated it to `inst-pk-window` plus two PRD reference tables (§17.1, §17.7). It reached **no FR, no AC and no DoD**: `fr-package-pricing` still enumerated package's required fields as `packageSize`/`packagePrice` only and closed with "publish MUST reject otherwise"; `fr-rating-compatibility` still said "**tiered** usage rows MUST additionally persist `tierAggregationWindow`"; §17.4's placement row said usage-row-only without naming the package case; AC #13 was scoped to tiered rows and AC #88/#89 — the package ACs — never mentioned the field. A requirement stated in one algorithm step, absent from the FR it implements and from every acceptance test, is a requirement an implementation drops silently, and the failure mode D-58 exists to prevent is a 15× charge spread on a published row.
- **Decision**: **(review fix), 2026-07-30** — the requirement is restated at every enforcement point: the FR (with the `billingGranularity`-does-not-bound-a-period rationale), the rating-compatibility contract, the §17.4 placement rule, AC #13 (scope widened to `package`), AC #88 (read model exposes three fields; the fold is over the window), AC #89 (missing window is a listed failure), AC #26, and both S3 DoDs. **Propagated**: PRD ×7 sites; S3 `dod-package` + `dod-tier-bands`.

#### D-71 [M] D-67 magnitude bounds propagated into the PRD

- **Where**: [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-priceoverlay-authoring`, AC #55, AC #26.
- **Problem**: D-67's landed half — `discount` bp ∈ `(0, 10000]`, `markup` bp > 0, amount magnitudes ≥ 0 — was recorded as propagated to "S9 schema column + §5 codes" only. The PRD kept describing an overlay magnitude as "a single basis-points value" with no range in `fr-priceoverlay-authoring`, AC #55 and §17.7, so the requirements document still permitted the exact data-entry inversion (`discount / percent_bp = 15000`, "150% of list") that the design now rejects. The direction is safe — the design is stricter — but the FR is what a reviewer reads to decide whether a validator is required, and a rule that exists only in a schema column is a rule an implementer may read as advisory.
- **Decision**: **(review fix), 2026-07-30** — the bounds and `ADJUSTMENT_MAGNITUDE_OUT_OF_RANGE` are stated in `fr-priceoverlay-authoring` and AC #55 with the "the `>= 0` money constraint binds price rows, not overlay lines" rationale, and the code joins AC #26. The **stacked-result floor** stays explicitly open (§F.1) and is named as such at both sites, so closing the authoring bound is not mistaken for closing the fork. **Propagated**: PRD `fr-priceoverlay-authoring`, AC #55, AC #26.

#### D-72 [L] `DESIGN.md` register summary brought current

- **Where**: [`DESIGN.md`](./DESIGN.md) §4 Additional context (decisions-register bullet); §1.3 design-set table rows for slices 09 and 10.
- **Problem**: the canonical index still reported "**all 39 decisions + 14 ratifications closed 2026-07-10**" — with D-40…D-68 decided since, seven items flagged for veto, one awaiting a countersign, one partially decided and two sections (§F.1, §F.2) of items deliberately **not** closed. A reader entering through `DESIGN.md`, which is the documented entry point, would conclude the register is settled. The slice table likewise described slice 09 without D-42's line containers and slice 10 without D-52/D-53/D-59/D-60.
- **Decision**: **(review fix), 2026-07-30** — the bullet now states the four waves, the live qualifiers (veto flags, the Billing countersign, the D-67 partial), and that §F.1/§F.2 are open; the two slice rows carry their governing decisions. **Propagated**: `DESIGN.md` §4 + §1.3 ×2 rows.

#### D-73 [H] `fr-invoice-currency-binding` narrowed to required add-ons and override targets

- **Where**: [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-invoice-currency-binding` clause (i), AC #80 clause (i); [`design/04-currency-tax.md`](./design/04-currency-tax.md) `inst-cb-addon` (unchanged).
- **Problem**: the PRD required publish/preview to reject "an add-on/override/**required**-add-on row lacking a row in a currency the base plan publishes" — an enumeration reading as *any* add-on. The 2026-07-28 review fix narrowed the design to **required** add-ons and price-override targets, moving an optional add-on's currency gap to attachment time (`inst-cb-addon`, flagged for veto), and the PRD was never moved. Two documents therefore disagreed about whether a base plan publishes: a plan with one optional add-on missing one market is publishable under S4 and blocked under the PRD.
- **Options**: (a) narrow the PRD to match the design; (b) withdraw the S4 carve-out and block on any gap.
- **Decision**: **(a), 2026-07-30 — flagged for veto** (it inherits `inst-cb-addon`'s flag). The invariant being protected is *one bound currency per invoice*, and an optional add-on that covers no row in the bound currency is **not attachable** on that market — it can never place a second currency on the invoice, so blocking the base plan's publish protects nothing and makes a plan's publishability depend on markets it does not sell in. (b) rejected on that ground. The attachment-time enforcement point (Subscriptions, against the sellability read) is named in the PRD so the requirement is not merely deleted. **Propagated**: PRD `fr-invoice-currency-binding`, AC #80.

#### D-74 [H] `fr-plan-phases` coverage narrowed to plans carrying a recurring part

- **Where**: [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-plan-phases`, AC #54, AC #26; [`design/02-plan-definition.md`](./design/02-plan-definition.md) `inst-ph-coverage` (unchanged).
- **Problem**: the PRD required **every** phase of **every** plan to be covered by ≥ 1 published **recurring** price row per sold `(currency, region)`. Taken literally that blocks every one-time and usage-only plan, which carry no recurring rows at all and still hold the D-19 implicit terminal phase — so the 2026-07-28 fix scoped `inst-ph-coverage` to `recurring`/`hybrid` plans, and the PRD was not moved. `inst-ph-terminal-stable` (D-64) states the consequence plainly: with the carve-out in place, usage-only plans are unguarded by this rule.
- **Options**: (a) narrow the PRD to match, relying on D-64 for the usage-only case; (b) withdraw the carve-out and author a coverage rule that also guards usage-only plans.
- **Decision**: **(a), 2026-07-30 — flagged for veto** (it inherits `inst-ph-coverage`'s flag). The PRD's unconditional MUST is unsatisfiable for two of the four billing cycles, so it cannot stand as written under either option; and the hazard it targets on usage-only plans — a phase that resolves to no row — is what D-64 now closes structurally, by making the terminal `phase_id` immutable across revisions and forbidding the drop of a phase a published row references. (b) remains the better long-run shape and is recorded as the reopen path: a coverage rule expressed over *the row kinds a plan actually carries* would subsume both, and D-64 would then be a redundant guard rather than the only one. **Propagated**: PRD `fr-plan-phases`, AC #54, AC #26 (which also gains the D-64 conditions, previously unlisted).

#### D-75 [L] AC #26 is declared illustrative, not exhaustive

- **Where**: [PRD](./PRD.md) AC #26 (new scope note).
- **Problem** (carried from §F.2): AC #26 is cited across the doc set as *the* aggregate fail-closed set, and D-08/D-18/D-42/D-43 each dutifully added their codes to it — while D-40, D-45, D-53, D-54 and D-55 did not. A list that is authoritative-by-citation and maintained by half its contributors is worse than either alternative: a reader checks it, finds a condition absent, and concludes the condition does not block.
- **Options**: (a) declare it illustrative and name the normative sources; (b) commit to maintaining it exhaustively (a check would have to enforce that every new publish-blocking condition lands there).
- **Decision**: **(a), 2026-07-30** — the normative fail-closed set is §17.4 plus every slice-registered pipeline rule; where AC #26 and a validator disagree, the validator governs, and absence from the list is never permission to publish. (b) rejected as unenforceable by hand at 100+ conditions across 12 slices; it becomes viable only with a mechanical check, which is the same tooling D-68's standing consequence asks for. **Propagated**: PRD AC #26 scope note.

#### D-76 [H] Legacy snapshot synthesis — disjoint reference store + a two-tier selection rule

- **Where**: [`design/05-governance.md`](./design/05-governance.md) `inst-bd-pipeline` + `inst-bd-store` (new) + §6 `pricing_historical_price` + `dod-backdating` + §9; [`design/11-lifecycle.md`](./design/11-lifecycle.md) `inst-sy-select` (new) + `inst-sy-provenance` + §6 + `dod-snapshot-synthesis` + §9; [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §3.7; [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-migration-safety`, `fr-historical-import-governance`, AC #58, AC #65.
- **Problem** (carried from §F.2 as [H]; two defects in one chain). (i) **Synthesis could not reach its own rows.** `inst-sy-freeze` resolves "published plan state as of the trigger instant", and published state in this gear is resolved **through windows** — the only effectivity mechanism. Backdated reference rows have no windows by construction (`inst-bd-noeffect` forbids the import from scheduling any), and `inst-bd-pipeline` confirms they are "resolved only via snapshot-synthesis provenance, never via window resolution" — naming a compensating mechanism that was specified nowhere. Tier (2) of the lookup was therefore unreachable. (ii) **The import feeding synthesis was self-rejecting.** `inst-bd-pipeline` ran scope-key uniqueness across `pricing_price`, so a historical price for a **still-published** plan collided with its live row — rejecting exactly the case synthesis exists for. Compounding both, the `lifecycle_state` of a reference row was never named, so it was neither inside nor outside the Foundation's partial `UNIQUE`.
- **Options**: (a) a separate `pricing_historical_price` store, disjoint from `pricing_price`; (b) an `origin` discriminator column on `pricing_price`, excluded from the index predicate, the read-model projection and the window checks; (c) forbid import onto a scope key that holds a current published row.
- **Decision**: **(a), 2026-07-30** — plus the missing selection rule, stated as a **two-tier lookup** (`inst-sy-select`): live/superseded row whose window covered `t` → else the reference row whose own `[effectiveFrom, effectiveTo)` covers `t` → else **fail closed**, never the current row; the provenance record carries the tier per resolved id. (c) rejected outright: it deletes the primary use case (a legacy subscription on a plan still being sold). (b) rejected on money-safety grounds, which is the whole argument between the two viable options: a reference row inside `pricing_price` turns **every** Foundation statement about published rows — §4.3 immutability, the partial `UNIQUE`, the REVOKE/trigger discipline, "consumers resolve only committed versions" — into a claim with an unstated exception class, and its containment then rests on every present and future query remembering a predicate. Disjoint stores make leakage impossible **by construction**; the cost is a duplicated column set and a union-free two-tier read, which is cheap by comparison. Uniqueness and interval non-overlap are scoped **within** the reference store, so a live row on the same scope key is no longer a conflict. **Propagated**: S5 ×5 sites (incl. the new store + 2 ACs); S11 ×5 sites (incl. `inst-sy-select` + 2 ACs); S1 §3.7 note; PRD ×4 sites. **Amended by D-81 (2026-07-30)**: the **temporal** half — the import's strictly-past bound made tier (2) unreachable for both trigger instants; reference `effective_to` may now reach `now` and beyond (open-ended), and the per-trigger `t` is restated design-side.

#### D-77 [M] Band unit on non-`sum` rows — `billingGranularity` MUST pair with `aggregationGranularity`

- **Where**: [`design/03-price-structure.md`](./design/03-price-structure.md) `inst-la-units` + `inst-la-granularity` (new) + §5 codes + `dod-level-aggregation` + schema note + §9; [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-level-aggregation`, AC #26.
- **Problem** (carried from §F.2 as [M]): two rules of one slice assign the tier-band unit and nothing reconciled them. `inst-tb-units` derives it from `billingGranularity` quantization; `inst-la-units` derives it from `level unit × aggregationGranularity` — and then resolved the conflict by deferring "per `inst-tb-units`", a circle. Nothing constrained the pair, so a `time_weighted` row with `aggregationGranularity = hour` and `billingGranularity = per_day` has bands in GB·h under one rule and GB·day under the other: a **24x error at the band edge**, passing every other stated check (`LEVEL_UNIT_MISMATCH` compares the SKU's declared unit against `level × granule`; it never compares the granule against `billingGranularity`).
- **Options**: (a) constrain the pair (`hour` ⇒ `per_hour`, `day` ⇒ `per_day`), else fail publish; (b) make `aggregationGranularity` authoritative on non-`sum` rows and forbid `billingGranularity` there; (c) state precedence only — `inst-la-units` wins, `billingGranularity` becomes documentation.
- **Decision**: **(a), 2026-07-30** — `LEVEL_GRANULARITY_MISMATCH` (422) on every other pairing, including `per_second`/`per_minute`/`whole_unit`. Both launch products fit (cloudlet peak-per-hour, storage GB-month time-weighted on an hourly granule). (b) is conceptually cleanest — on a level row the fold *is* the quantization — but `billingGranularity` is REQUIRED on all usage rows by `fr-rating-compatibility` and AC #14, so it needs a PRD carve-out and touches the Rating contract for no additional safety. (c) rejected on the doc set's own doctrine: it leaves a field the author sets and the system ignores, which is precisely what `TIER_QUAL_ON_NON_TIERED` was widened to forbid ("an accepted-but-ignored value would mask authoring errors"). With the pairing pinned the two rules name one unit **by construction**, so `inst-tb-units` needs no exception. **Propagated**: S3 ×6 sites; PRD `fr-level-aggregation` + AC #26.

#### D-78 [M] Overlay lines carry an optional `cohort` filter; grandfathered rows are exempt by default

- **Where**: [`design/09-price-overlays.md`](./design/09-price-overlays.md) `inst-plv-eligibility` (new) + `pricing_price_overlay_line` schema + §5 codes + `dod-priceoverlay` + §9; [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-priceoverlay-authoring`, AC #55.
- **Problem** (open product fork, §F.1): the overlay line key is `(planId, targetSku)` with no eligibility or cohort axis, and the stack applies to "the resolved base rows" with nothing excluding `priceEligibility = existing_grandfathered`. The whole ADR-0002 machinery works — the row immutable in price, its window live, its generation selected by the pinned price id — and a single `+2000 bp` markup line still moves the effective charge of a cohort that was guaranteed its price, **without mutating any row**. The documents simultaneously promised price stability and permitted the move.
- **Options**: (a) exempt grandfathered rows unless a line explicitly names the cohort; (b) status quo, stated explicitly (the guarantee covers the base row, not the effective price); (c) direction-limited — only non-increasing adjustments reach grandfathered rows.
- **Decision**: **(a), 2026-07-30 — joint with Rating** (the filter is adopted verbatim, like most-specific-wins and the class tie-break). The `cohort` column is an **eligibility filter, not a specificity level**: NULL ⇒ the line applies only to `all_subscriptions`/`new_subscriptions_only` rows, a value ⇒ only to that generation — so the existing `(planId, targetSku)` > `(planId)` > default ordering runs unchanged **within** the eligible set and no resolution semantics move. An unknown cohort fails publish (`OVERLAY_LINE_COHORT_UNKNOWN`). (b) rejected: it is coherent only if the "price stays stable" language is withdrawn from the grandfathering sections, which is a worse trade than one nullable column. (c) rejected as **undecidable in isolation** — "non-increasing" is not computable while the `fixed` arithmetic fork (replace vs subtract) is open, so it would couple this decision to two other unresolved forks and to the stack sort direction. (a) is the only option that closes the hazard without depending on them. **Propagated**: S9 ×5 sites; PRD `fr-priceoverlay-authoring` + AC #55. **Owed**: the Rating-side adoption of the filter in its overlay-precedence step.

#### D-79 [H] The "in-flight subscribers per scope key" predicate has no data source

- **Where**: [PRD](./PRD.md) §9.2 `contract-subscriptions` (a third inbound lane) + the retirement story's "View the active subscription count"; [`design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md`](./design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md) `inst-fg-trailing`; [`design/11-lifecycle.md`](./design/11-lifecycle.md) `inst-rt-cancel`; [`design/05-governance.md`](./design/05-governance.md) `inst-mat-registered`.
- **Problem** (2026-07-30 slice review, H-1): D-51 keeps continuing-coverage windows "only for scope keys with **no in-flight subscribers**", D-62 exempts window cancel/shorten from both the trailing-void check and the materiality trigger on the same predicate, and the retirement confirm screen must label kept vs cancelled windows per key — but pricing owns no subscription data by design, and §9.2 declares exactly **two** inbound Subscriptions lanes (grandfathering re-bind feedback; the D-65 migration handshake), neither of which carries a per-scope-key subscriber count. The PRD's own retirement story opens with "View the active subscription count" and no lane supplies it. This is the defect class D-01 and D-65 were closed on: the rule is written, the input does not exist.
- **Decision**: **(review fix), 2026-07-30 — flagged for veto · joint with Subscriptions** — a **third inbound lane**: per-scope-key in-flight-subscription presence. Pricing submits the price-id set of the canonical scope key(s) under evaluation (the pinned price id is what Subscriptions holds, via `pricingSnapshotRef`); Subscriptions returns the count of **non-terminal** subscriptions whose pinned snapshot references any of them (the plan-level total of the retirement story is the same call over all the plan's price ids). Consumed at: retirement dry-run + commit (D-51), window cancel/shorten validation + materiality routing (D-62), and the retirement confirm screen. **Fail-closed on outage**: an unavailable or timed-out lane is treated as *subscribers present* — windows kept, gap check + approval required (the conservative direction; mirrors `inst-cl-source`). The predicate is **re-resolved inside the mutating commit**; the residual read-to-commit race is bounded and backstopped by D-80's coverage-horizon predicate and reconciliation alarm. **Propagated**: PRD §9.2 (now three inbound lanes); S7 `inst-fg-trailing` (predicate source, normative home); S11 `inst-rt-cancel` + §10 risks; S5 `inst-mat-registered` (cite); subscriptions SEAMS **SUB-P8** (new). **Owed**: the Subscriptions-side presence read (indexed by pinned price id) before design freeze.

#### D-80 [M] The D-51/D-62 exemption races the sellability gate — the trailing void reopened for future subscribers

- **Where**: [`design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md`](./design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md) `inst-fg-trailing` + `inst-sg-surface` + §7 alarms + DoD + §9; [`design/05-governance.md`](./design/05-governance.md) `inst-mat-registered`; [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-sellability-gate` + §9.2.
- **Problem** (2026-07-30 slice review, M-1): the exemption evaluates "no in-flight subscribers" at cancel time, but selling continues — the gate is six point-in-time predicates and **future coverage is not one of them**. A key with active window W1 (ends `t2`), an approved scheduled successor W2 and zero subscribers: cancelling W2 passes exempt (no trailing-void check, no approval), the plan stays sellable until `t2`, and anyone subscribing in `[cancel, t2)` lands in the trailing void — arrears and renewals fail closed. The exact hazard class D-62 closed, reopened through its own exemption.
- **Decision**: **(review fix), 2026-07-30 — flagged for veto · joint with Subscriptions** — three parts. (1) The exemption narrows: it applies only when the key has no in-flight subscribers **and is not currently sellable** — on a sellable key the trailing-void check and the materiality trigger always apply, so cancelling the sole successor of a sellable key is simply rejected (`WINDOW_TRAILING_VOID`). (2) Sellability predicate (1) gains a **coverage horizon**: the key's active-plus-scheduled coverage MUST extend through `now + the longest billing cycle sold on the key` (an open-ended window satisfies it trivially) — the same margin D-04 mandates for grandfathered copies and `inst-fg-trailing` uses for cancels. The predicate count stays six; the surface additionally exposes the per-key coverage end. A finite-covered key (via an exempted cancel, or simply authored so) therefore stops selling one full cycle before its coverage ends — nobody can buy into a void. (3) A reconciliation alarm, `pricing.window.coverage_ending_with_subscribers` (Warn), fires when the D-79 lane reports in-flight subscribers on a key whose remaining coverage ends within that horizon — the operational backstop for the bounded read-to-commit race and for pre-existing finite coverage with subscribers aboard (renewals are not gate-checked, `inst-sg-renewal`; their tail is owned by supersession/migration mechanics, and this alarm is what makes the approaching end visible). **Propagated**: S7 ×3 instructions + §7 + DoD + AC; S5 `inst-mat-registered`; PRD `fr-sellability-gate` + the §9.2 gate line; subscriptions SEAMS SUB-P5 (the adopted gate now includes the horizon).

#### D-81 [H] Synthesis tier 2 was unreachable — the temporal half of D-76

- **Where**: [`design/05-governance.md`](./design/05-governance.md) `inst-bd-noeffect` + §6 `pricing_historical_price` + `dod-backdating` + §9; [`design/11-lifecycle.md`](./design/11-lifecycle.md) M4 + `inst-sy-freeze` + `dod-snapshot-synthesis` + §9; [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-migration-safety`, `fr-historical-import-governance`.
- **Problem** (2026-07-30 slice review, H-2): three rules were mutually unsatisfiable. `inst-bd-noeffect` rejects any reference row whose effective dates "reach `now` or later" or whose range intersects a not-yet-closed billing period; synthesis freezes state "as of the trigger instant" (M4) — and the PRD AC already defines that instant per trigger: the **migration effective timestamp** (future at scheduling, under the D-49 notice floor) or the **earliest unrated usage timestamp** (inside an open billing period by definition). Tier 2 of D-76's rule needs a reference row covering that instant, but every legally imported row is strictly past and closed-period — so tier 2 never fires. D-13's purpose ("backdated rows shape `migrated-origin` snapshots that rating consumes going forward") was unconstructible: a legacy plan no longer sold failed closed forever; a plan still sold resolved tier 1 at the **current** price, not the legacy price synthesis exists to reconstruct. D-76 fixed the structural half; the temporal half survived it.
- **Options**: (a) make the synthesis `t` historical; (b) let reference intervals extend to `now` and beyond.
- **Decision**: **(b) + a design↔PRD alignment, 2026-07-30 — flagged for veto**. The design restates the **per-trigger `t`** the PRD AC already defines (M4 and `inst-sy-freeze` had flattened it to "the trigger instant"; (a) alone would still fail the migration trigger, whose `t` is future-of-scheduling). The import constraint splits: `effective_from` MUST stay **strictly past** (a wholly-future reference row is future authoring and belongs to the publish path), while `effective_to` may be any later instant **including `now`-or-later and open-ended (`null`)** — the legacy price book's standing price is importable as still-in-effect. The not-yet-closed-billing-period rejection is dropped: it guarded against retroactive effect back when reference rows would have sat beside live rows, and D-76's disjoint store now guarantees structural inertness (never window-linked, never projected, never a coverage/sellability/addressability input; sole reader `inst-sy-select`), while the sanctioned **forward** effect — shaping `migrated-origin` snapshots — is exactly what D-13's always-material two-person approval governs. `BACKDATE_SIDE_EFFECT` narrows accordingly (`effective_from ≥ now`, window scheduling, warm-events); in-store non-overlap treats `null` as +∞. **Propagated**: S5 ×4 sites + AC; S11 ×3 sites + AC (tier 2 exercised end-to-end on an open-ended row); PRD ×2 sites; the D-76 entry carries a pointer here.

#### D-82 [H] Supersession may not change the fields the continued `Q` is denominated in

- **Where**: [`design/03-price-structure.md`](./design/03-price-structure.md) `inst-tb-supersession-units` (new) + §5 codes + `dod-tier-bands` + fixture registry + §9; [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §4.3; [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-supersession` + §17.5.
- **Problem** (2026-07-30 slice review, H-3): `inst-tb-window-continuity` continues the tier counter across supersession ("the new row's bands are simply applied to the continued `Q`"), while supersession constrains only the scope key, one eligibility class and one `chargeKind` — none of `meter`, `billingGranularity`, `aggregationFunction`/`aggregationGranularity`, `tierAggregationWindow` is a scope-key axis, so a successor could change any of them mid-window. A `per_hour` → `per_day` supersession applies an hours-denominated continued `Q` to day-denominated bands — the same ×24 band-edge class D-77 closed for level rows, reintroduced through supersession; a `meter` change silently reads a different counter stream.
- **Options**: (a) publish check — the successor preserves the unit/counter-determining fields; (b) define such a change as an explicit counter reset.
- **Decision**: **(a), 2026-07-30** — on a **usage-row** supersession the successor MUST carry the predecessor's `meter`, `dimensionKey`, `billingGranularity`, `aggregationFunction`, `aggregationGranularity`, `tierAggregationWindow` and `tierQualificationWindow` unchanged; any difference fails publish (`SUPERSESSION_UNIT_MISMATCH`, 422, offending fields named). Supersession is a **price** change on one key; changing what or how the key meters is a **structural** change and routes through plan revisioning + migration — which is what the §17.5 mechanism taxonomy already says. (b) rejected: a mid-window reset is a commercial semantics change (`subscription_lifetime` counters especially), and `inst-tb-window-continuity`'s single invariant — supersession never resets `Q` — is worth keeping unconditional. The supersession-continuity fixture gains the negative scenario. **Propagated**: S3 ×5 sites; S1 §4.3 clause; PRD `fr-supersession` + §17.5. **Amended by D-98 (2026-07-31)**: `model_kind` joins the preserved set on usage rows (a graduated→volume/package flip mid-window re-prices the accumulated window total under new math); **extended to the phase axis by D-89** (a phase-scoped usage override preserves the same fields — the counter is phase-blind). **`package_size` joins the set by D-122 (2026-07-31)** — block math is non-linear in the window and the T-D-12 cumulative ceil-diff presupposes one block size per window.

#### D-83 [M] The draft-revision model extends to the plan's child tables (D-56 completion)

- **Where**: [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §3.7 + §4.3 + §4.4; [`design/02-plan-definition.md`](./design/02-plan-definition.md) §6 (`pricing_plan_phase`, `pricing_plan_addon_rule`, `pricing_plan_descriptor_set`) + AC.
- **Problem** (2026-07-30 slice review, M-2): D-56 gave `pricing_plan` `(plan_id, revision)` rows, but a phase row was a **single row per `phase_id`** mutated on re-attach, and the add-on-rule / descriptor-set tables had no revision dimension at all. Editing draft revision N+1 therefore mutated the truth-side state of published revision N (two compositions had nowhere to coexist), and a degraded-warm **re-drive** could re-project revision N with N+1's draft edits — "consumers never read draft" violated through a side door — while the projection source for re-warm (truth tables vs a frozen payload) was specified nowhere.
- **Decision**: **(review fix), 2026-07-30 — flagged for veto** (inherits D-56's flag) — child rows **version with the revision, copy-on-new-revision**: `pricing_plan_phase` is keyed `(phase_id, plan_revision)` — the `phase_id` half stays stable across revisions, so the `phase` scope-key axis and same-key supersession are untouched (exactly D-56's promise, now storable) — and `pricing_plan_addon_rule` / `pricing_plan_descriptor_set` carry `plan_revision` the same way. A published revision's child rows are immutable with it; the open draft revision edits its own copies; the terminal-phase partial `UNIQUE` on `(plan_id, plan_revision)` becomes coherent. The re-warm **projection source** is stated normatively: the projector — initial warm and re-drive alike — reads the **published revision's own rows**, never the open draft's, so a re-drive after a degraded publish cannot leak draft edits. **Propagated**: S1 §3.7 / §4.3 / §4.4; S2 §6 ×3 tables + AC. **Extended by D-92 (2026-07-31)**: the same copy-on-new-revision discipline reaches the S8 bundle child tables and the S9 overlay tables (draft-revision rows).

#### D-84 [M] A hybrid's usage part must exist per sold market — and per priced line

- **Where**: [`design/02-plan-definition.md`](./design/02-plan-definition.md) `inst-cs-hybrid` + `inst-cs-usage` + §5 codes + `dod-billing-cycles` + AC; [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-hybrid-completeness`.
- **Problem** (2026-07-30 slice review, M-3): per-market completeness is required for one-time and recurring rows, phase coverage is recurring-only (D-74), and hybrid completeness was "≥ 1 usage row **anywhere**" — no rule required usage rows per market (bundles, by contrast, get explicit per-market component coverage). Sellability evaluates the keys that exist, so an absent usage key fails nothing: a hybrid with recurring in EUR+USD and usage only in EUR was sellable in USD, and the USD subscriber's usage events failed closed — the "sold but unrateable" state D-15/D-17 declare impossible by construction. The same asymmetry exists one level down (meter M1 priced in both markets, M2 in one) and on usage-only plans.
- **Decision**: **(review fix), 2026-07-30** — publish check `USAGE_MARKET_INCOMPLETE` (422, meter/dimension/market named): every `(meter, dimensionKey)` line the plan prices — evaluated over its **phase-invariant terminal-phase rows** (phase-scoped overrides are additive and exempt) — MUST have a published usage row for **every sold `(currency, region)` of the plan**; sold markets are the recurring rows' markets on `recurring`/`hybrid` plans and the union of usage-row markets on usage-only plans. A market where usage is genuinely free is an explicit `$0`-amount row (Q5), never an absence. **Propagated**: S2 ×4 sites; PRD `fr-hybrid-completeness`.

#### D-85 [M] Drift flags are operator-plane state, never the versioned read model

- **Where**: [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §3.7 + §4.4; [`design/02-plan-definition.md`](./design/02-plan-definition.md) `inst-cmp-tier-drift`; [`design/06-consumer-contracts.md`](./design/06-consumer-contracts.md) `inst-gs-drift`; [`design/04-currency-tax.md`](./design/04-currency-tax.md) `inst-td-readiness`.
- **Problem** (2026-07-30 slice review, M-4): `tier_divergent`, `grants_divergent` and the tax-readiness divergence flag published plans "**in the read model**" on an external registry / Tax-Engine signal — but the read model is monotonic per `CatalogVersion` and advances only through publish units, and a flag has no publish unit (the exact problem D-06 solved for overlays/memberships; the flags never received the treatment). Either the flag mutates a frozen version in place (doctrine violation — a pinned consumer sees the version change mid-run) or it waits for an unrelated publish (unbounded lag). The overlay flags (`coverage_incomplete`, `target_retired`) are **not** affected: their causing mutation is itself a publish unit they ride.
- **Options**: (a) an operator-plane store outside the versioned read model; (b) each drift signal becomes its own publish unit (the D-06 pattern).
- **Decision**: **(a), 2026-07-30** — the flags are remediation signals for operators, not consumer-resolution inputs (all three sites already state that consumers keep resolving the frozen values), so they gain nothing from version addressability, and (b) would spend a `CatalogVersion` + a warm per external signal to deliver information no pinned consumer reads. New Foundation-owned table **`pricing_operator_flag`** (`(tenant_id, subject_ref, flag)`, set/cleared by the signal-consuming handlers, audited), surfaced on authoring/operator reads (`plan × read`) and via the existing alarms; `pricing_read_model` **never** carries operator flags. **Propagated**: S1 §3.7 + §4.4; S2 / S6 / S4 one instruction each.

#### D-86 [M] Read-model storage is a per-plan delta; retention rides the truth-history horizon

- **Where**: [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §3.7 `pricing_read_model` + §4.4.
- **Problem** (2026-07-30 slice review, performance): `pricing_read_model` is keyed `(tenant_id, catalog_version, plan_id)` with nothing saying whether a version stores a **full tenant copy** or a **delta**, and no retention statement at all — while replay/reproducibility require old versions to stay resolvable for years. With ≤ 5s interactive coalescing a full copy per publish explodes storage; a delta changes the read contract. Either way it shapes the schema and the read contract, so it had to be decided before code.
- **Decision**: **(review fix), 2026-07-30** — **per-plan delta**: a version's rows are exactly the plans of the publishes that produced it; resolving `(pin V, plan P)` reads P's row with the **greatest `catalog_version ≤ V` whose warm-completion marker is set** — a single indexed read on `(tenant_id, plan_id, catalog_version DESC)`, inside the p95 < 100ms budget. Monotonicity is unchanged: a version participates only once committed + warm-complete, and completed versions never mutate. Retention: delta rows are retained on the **same horizon as the append-only truth history** (≥ 7y, jurisdiction-configurable, aligned with the audit store) — growth is O(publishes), the same order as `pricing_price` itself, which is what makes indefinite retention affordable (the full-copy model is what made it explosive); compacting superseded deltas beyond the horizon is an ops knob, never a semantics change. **Propagated**: S1 §3.7 + §4.4. **Amended by D-91 (2026-07-31)**: the delta store is keyed per **subject** (`(tenant_id, catalog_version, subject_kind, subject_ref)`) so the D-06 overlay/membership publish units are representable; plans keep these exact semantics as the `plan` subject kind.

#### D-87 [H] Tier-2 synthesis output was not consumable — self-contained `migrated-origin` payload + reference-row band storage

- **Where**: [`design/05-governance.md`](./design/05-governance.md) §6 `pricing_historical_price` + `inst-bd-pipeline`; [`design/11-lifecycle.md`](./design/11-lifecycle.md) `inst-sy-select`/`inst-sy-provenance` + §6 + DoD + AC; [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §4.4; [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-migration-safety`, `fr-historical-import-governance`.
- **Problem** (2026-07-31 slice review, H-1): two gaps left D-76/D-81's reachable tier 2 unusable. (i) `pricing_historical_price` declared no tier-band or package storage while the row-shape subset requires a `graduated`/`volume` reference row to carry ≥ 1 band (S3 `inst-mk-required`) — a tiered legacy price was unimportable by its own validator. (ii) The synthesized snapshot recorded resolved price **ids**, but a tier-2 id lives in a store that is "never projected, never in a `CatalogVersion`, sole reader = synthesis" — while rating must "retry against the frozen result" and charge from modelKind/bands/eval-policy it can read nowhere; which `CatalogVersion` a `migrated-origin` ref pins (a tier-1 row resolved at a historical `t` predates any retained version mapping) was likewise unstated. The D-01 defect class: the rule is written, the consumption mechanism does not exist.
- **Decision**: **(review fix), 2026-07-31** — (i) the reference store carries the **full evaluable content**: `tier_bands` (ordered band array, validated by the same S3 geometry rules) and `package_size`/`package_price_minor`; the row-shape subset validates them like authored rows. (ii) A `migrated-origin` snapshot is **self-contained**: synthesis materializes the complete evaluable row content — `model_kind`, ordered bands / package fields, evaluation-policy + S6 consumer-contract fields, `tax_inclusive`, the resolved rounding policy — into the frozen payload beside the ids + selection tier, for tier 1 and tier 2 alike; Rating/Tariffs evaluate **from the payload** and never resolve a `migrated-origin` id through the read model, so no `CatalogVersion` pin is required or recorded (the payload is the replay source; posted periods re-query nothing). D-76's isolation claim survives verbatim — the store's only reader is still synthesis.
- **Propagated**: S5 §6 + `inst-bd-pipeline` + `dod-backdating`; S11 `inst-sy-payload` (new) + `inst-sy-provenance` + §6 + DoD + AC; S1 §4.4; PRD `fr-migration-safety` + `fr-historical-import-governance`.

#### D-88 [H] The supersession change unit — composition, API, and the changeover-instant floor (interactive and bulk)

- **Where**: [`design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md`](./design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md) (new supersession flow/algo + API + codes + DoD + AC); [`design/03-price-structure.md`](./design/03-price-structure.md) `inst-ps-supersede`; [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §4.3; [`design/12-operator-efficiency.md`](./design/12-operator-efficiency.md) `inst-mr-api`/`inst-mr-apply`; [`design/05-governance.md`](./design/05-governance.md) endpoint map; [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-supersession`, §17.5.
- **Problem** (2026-07-31 slice review, H-2 + M-5): the set's most common change mechanism had no defined operation. The "supersession unit" was *named* everywhere (one-pending-unit-per-key, D-35 key pinning, the S5 approval hash) but nothing specified what composes one; assembled from the documented primitives it is unbuildable-or-broken — the successor's window collides with the predecessor's open-ended window (`WINDOW_OVERLAP`); shortening the predecessor first is its own always-material D-62 operation and drops the key below the D-80 coverage horizon (a sales outage) until the successor schedules; two approvals instead of one. Separately, the cutover's D-47 instant floor (`CUTOVER_INSTANT_PASSED`, the 2026-07-30 L-4 fix) had no supersession analogue: a changeover at commit time activates the successor's window while its row is not yet addressable in any completed `CatalogVersion` — up to 5 minutes of transient fail-closed renewals/arrears, multiplied across every key of a repricing run (bulk = the 5-minute hard max).
- **Decision**: **(review fix), 2026-07-31** — the **supersession unit**, mirroring the cutover. `POST /bss-pricing/v1/plans/{planId}/supersessions` composes (a) the successor draft row on the same canonical scope key, (b) the predecessor window's `effectiveTo` shorten to the changeover instant, (c) the successor window scheduled from that instant — validated gap-free at compose, submitted as **one approval unit** (pends the key per `inst-co-single-pending`), committed as **one local ACID transaction** (D-03) that re-runs the pipeline, flips the predecessor `published → superseded`, and applies both window operations — no interim state exists in which the key is shortened without its successor, so neither `WINDOW_OVERLAP` nor the coverage horizon is ever transiently violated. The changeover instant MUST be strictly future at submit **and ≥ the max batching-delay SLO (D-47: 5 min) in the future at approval commit** (`SUPERSESSION_INSTANT_PASSED`, 422; the unit is recomposed). **Bulk**: a mass-repricing run names **one changeover instant** for all its rows, bounded the same way against the run's approval commit; the per-row supersessions inside the run use it. Idempotency per `(planId, scope key, changeover instant)`.
- **Propagated**: S7 flow/algo/names/API/codes/DoD/AC; S3 `inst-ps-supersede`; S1 §4.3; S12 `inst-mr-api` + `inst-mr-apply` + AC; S5 endpoint map; PRD `fr-supersession` + §17.5. **Amended 2026-07-31**: **D-100** — naming this unit the *only* path to `published → superseded` made the grandfathering cutover uncommittable (its successor shares the predecessor's key), so the cutover is stated as the second sanctioned producer of the flip; **D-111** — "the commit re-runs the pipeline" is scoped for bulk runs (row-local rules + the touched key's window checks per row; the plan-level aggregate pass once per plan per run), since a literal per-row aggregate re-run makes a repricing run O(rows × plan-validation cost) against an SLO ratified before this decision.

#### D-89 [H] Phase-scoped usage overrides preserve the unit/counter fields — the D-82 guard extended to the phase axis

- **Where**: [`design/02-plan-definition.md`](./design/02-plan-definition.md) `inst-ph-usage-invariant` + `inst-ph-override-units` (new) + §5 code + DoD + AC; [`design/03-price-structure.md`](./design/03-price-structure.md) `inst-tb-window-continuity`; [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-plan-phases`.
- **Problem** (2026-07-31 slice review, H-3): the tier counter is keyed `(subscription, meter, dimensionKey, window)` — **phase-blind** — so `Q` continues across a phase conversion; yet nothing constrained a phase-scoped usage override's `billingGranularity`/`aggregationFunction`/`aggregationGranularity`/`tierAggregationWindow`/`tierQualificationWindow` (or `model_kind`) against the terminal-phase row it overrides. A `per_hour` trial row converting into a `per_day` evergreen row mid-window applies an hours-denominated continued `Q` to day-denominated bands — the D-77/D-82 ×24 class through a third door — and where the window values differ the counter silently resets; neither continuation nor reset was stated anywhere.
- **Decision**: **(review fix), 2026-07-31** — (a) the counter's phase-blindness is stated normatively: a phase conversion **never** resets `Q` (the counter belongs to the subscription's usage history; `subscription_lifetime` counters especially survive conversions — the same invariant supersession carries). (b) Publish check: a phase-scoped usage override MUST carry the same unit/counter-determining fields as the phase-invariant terminal-phase row of its `(meter, dimensionKey)` line — the D-82 list plus `model_kind` (D-98) — else `PHASE_OVERRIDE_UNIT_MISMATCH` (422, offending fields named). Free-trial pricing stays fully expressible (a `$0` rate or band set at the same denomination). The supersession-continuity fixture gains a phase-conversion-mid-window scenario.
- **Propagated**: S2 `inst-ph-usage-invariant` + `inst-ph-override-units` + §5 + DoD + AC; S3 `inst-tb-window-continuity`; PRD `fr-plan-phases`.

#### D-90 [M] Plan revisions get flip-at-commit — `published → superseded`, one current revision per plan

- **Where**: [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §3.7 `pricing_plan` + §4.3; [`design/02-plan-definition.md`](./design/02-plan-definition.md) §4 state machine + AC; [`design/11-lifecycle.md`](./design/11-lifecycle.md) `inst-rt-cancel`.
- **Problem** (2026-07-31 slice review, M-1): `pricing_price` predecessors flip `published → superseded` at the successor's commit (the 2026-07-28 fix the partial `UNIQUE` rests on), but plan **revision** rows had no analogue — after revision N+1 publishes, revision N stays `published` forever. "Current revision" was undefined at the storage level, retire's target row unstated, and any truth-side `EXISTS(lifecycle_state = 'published')` query (overlay referential integrity, migration-target checks, bundle-component checks) could match a stale revision — the remembered-predicate hazard D-76 exists to eliminate, rebuilt into the revision model.
