- **Decision**: **(review fix), 2026-07-31** — the predecessor revision row flips **`published → superseded`** inside the new revision's publish commit; a partial `UNIQUE (plan_id) WHERE lifecycle_state = 'published'` holds at most one **current** revision (mirroring the price-row index); retire flips the single current revision `published → retired`; superseded revision rows are immutable history like superseded price rows. "The published revision" — the D-83 projection source, the sellability lifecycle predicate, every referential check — is thereby unique **by construction**.
- **Propagated**: S1 §3.7 + §4.3; S2 §4 (`inst-pl-supersede` new) + AC; S11 `inst-rt-cancel`.

#### D-91 [M] The read-model delta store is keyed per subject — the D-06 publish units become representable (D-86 amendment)

- **Where**: [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §3.7 `pricing_read_model` + §4.4; [`design/09-price-overlays.md`](./design/09-price-overlays.md) §7 + `inst-pl-commit`/`inst-gm-return`.
- **Problem** (2026-07-31 slice review, M-2): D-06 made every overlay/membership mutation its own publish unit; D-86 keyed the delta store `(tenant_id, catalog_version, plan_id)`. Nobody reconciled them: a membership is per payer (no plan at all), and a `global`/`brand`/`region` overlay targets many or all plans — projected as per-plan deltas, one global-overlay commit re-projects the whole tenant, exactly the explosion D-86 exists to forbid; "publishes membership into the read model" had no defined keying or surface at all.
- **Decision**: **(review fix), 2026-07-31** — `pricing_read_model` is keyed **`(tenant_id, catalog_version, subject_kind, subject_ref)`**, `subject_kind ∈ {plan, price_overlay, group_membership}` (extensible). A plan publish projects its plan-subject row exactly as D-86 defined — nothing changes for plans; an overlay publish unit projects **one overlay-subject row** (the overlay document: lines, amounts, dating, disclosure, lifecycle) and **never re-projects targeted plans** — Tariffs joins overlays to base rows at evaluation, per the existing §9.2 contract; a membership publish unit projects one membership-subject row per payer record. Resolution per subject is the same greatest-completed-≤-pin rule; the warm-completion marker is per subject row (which also settles the per-version-vs-per-row marker ambiguity: a version's subjects become resolvable independently as their warms complete).
- **Propagated**: S1 §3.7 + §4.4; S9 §7 + `inst-pl-commit` + `inst-gm-return`; the D-86 entry carries the amendment pointer. **Amended by D-101 (2026-07-31)**: the per-subject marker stays, but **pin-eligibility is version-level** — this entry's "subjects resolve independently as their warms complete, each falling back to its own prior completed delta meanwhile" made a *single* pin resolve two contents over time; the fallback is now unobservable at a pin-eligible version. **Extended by D-112 (2026-07-31)**: an overlay publish unit also re-projects a tenant `overlay_index` subject, because per-subject resolution gave overlay *enumeration* no access path.

#### D-92 [M] Revision discipline for bundle child tables and overlays (D-83 completion, second pass)

- **Where**: [`design/08-bundles.md`](./design/08-bundles.md) §6 ×3 tables + AC; [`design/09-price-overlays.md`](./design/09-price-overlays.md) §6 (overlay tables) + AC.
- **Problem** (2026-07-31 slice review, M-3): D-83 versioned S2's three child tables and stopped. `pricing_bundle_component`/`pricing_bundle_revshare`/`pricing_bundle_revshare_group` are keyed by `bundle_id` alone, and `pricing_price_overlay*` carry no `lifecycle_state`/revision at all (while the submit surface is idempotent "per revision") — a draft recomposition of a published bundle, or a draft edit of a published overlay, mutates the published truth state, and a degraded-warm re-drive can leak the draft into a frozen version: the exact D-83 defect class, two slices over ("record the rule, skip the enforcement point").
- **Decision**: **(review fix), 2026-07-31** — (a) bundles ride the plan revision: the three bundle child tables gain `plan_revision` (copy-on-new-revision, D-83 semantics verbatim; a published revision's composition is immutable with it). (b) Overlays get **draft-revision rows**: `pricing_price_overlay` keyed `(price_overlay_id, revision)` with `lifecycle_state` (`draft | published | superseded`), a partial `UNIQUE` one open draft, and flip-at-commit per D-90; lines and amounts carry the owning `overlay_revision` (copy-on-new-revision); the published revision's rows are immutable and are the projection source for warm and re-drive alike.
- **Propagated**: S8 §6 + AC; S9 §6 ×3 tables + AC; the D-83 entry carries the extension pointer. **Corrected and extended 2026-07-31**: **D-105** restores the row discriminators this entry's "keyed `(bundle_id, plan_revision)`" phrasing dropped (the keys as written held one component / one rev-share party / one group per revision); **D-106** carries the same discipline to the last two plan children (`pricing_plan_grant`, `pricing_composite_meter`); **D-107** makes the overlay `precedence` and interval-overlap checks revision-scoped, which the revisions introduced here required and did not receive.

#### D-93 [M] Change-edge boundary classification moves to read time (D-25 revision)

- **Where**: [`design/06-consumer-contracts.md`](./design/06-consumer-contracts.md) `inst-pc-boundary` + §6 `allowed_change_targets`; [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-plan-change-contract`, AC #108.
- **Problem** (2026-07-31 slice review, M-4): D-25 stamped `in_place | cancel_plus_new` on the edge at the **source's** publish and promised re-computation "on either side's re-publish" — but a target's publish unit warms only the target's own delta (D-86/D-91) and the source's published revision is immutable, so no re-computation mechanism exists or can exist under the frozen read model; a stale `in_place` lets Subscriptions run an in-place change across a currency/region/frequency boundary (wrong proration — money).
- **Decision**: **(review fix), 2026-07-31 — flagged for veto · joint with Subscriptions** (it revises D-25's mechanism and the PRD FR). The classification is computed **at change time by Subscriptions** from both plans' published facts at its pinned version — the target covers the subscription's frozen `(currency, region)` with matching frequency ⇒ `in_place`, else `cancel_plus_new` — exactly the read-time discipline `comparabilityRank` already uses (both ranks resolve at read; D-54 guards their presence). The catalog publishes the inputs (each plan's market/frequency facts; the edge list) and the disclosure obligation stands (credit forfeiture disclosed before execution); the stamped `boundaryClass` leaves the edge payload. Publish-time validation of the edge (target published, rank present) is unchanged.
- **Propagated**: S6 `inst-pc-boundary` + §6; PRD glossary + `fr-plan-change-contract` + AC #108; subscriptions SEAMS SUB-P1 (the adopted read-time rule).

#### D-94 [M] The sellability gate is a conjunction over every key the purchase binds; the D-80/D-62 exemption evaluates plan-market sellability

- **Where**: [`design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md`](./design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md) `inst-sg-surface`/`inst-sg-joint` + `inst-fg-trailing` + DoD + AC; [`design/05-governance.md`](./design/05-governance.md) `inst-mat-registered`; [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-sellability-gate`.
- **Problem** (2026-07-31 slice review, M-6): the gate was stated "for the bound canonical scope key" — singular — while a hybrid/phased plan binds several keys at purchase (recurring + usage lines + setup; the phase chain); the conjunction was explicit only for bundles (`inst-sg-bundle`). Consequences: which key a storefront must evaluate was undefined, and the D-80 exemption ("no in-flight subscribers **and** not currently sellable") was undecidable for a component key — an exempt cancel of a usage-key window on a zero-subscriber hybrid whose recurring key stayed sellable reopened the trailing void for the usage line (sold-but-unrateable, the state D-84 closes at publish, reopened through window mutation).
- **Decision**: **(review fix), 2026-07-31 — flagged for veto · joint with Subscriptions** — the gate evaluates the **conjunction over every scope key the purchase would bind on the bound `(currency, region)`**: each `(phase, chargeKind, meter-line)` key the plan publishes there, eligibility-resolved (`new_subscriptions_only` wins over `all_subscriptions` where both exist; grandfathered generations are never gate inputs) — predicates (1)–(5) plus the D-80 coverage horizon **per key**, predicate (6) on the offered SKU. One failing component key makes the plan-market not sellable — never partially sellable (`inst-sg-bundle` stays the bundle-specific statement of the same rule). The D-62/D-80 **exemption** correspondingly evaluates **the plan's sellability on the key's market**: cancelling any component key's window of a currently-sellable plan-market is rejected (`WINDOW_TRAILING_VOID`), zero subscribers or not.
- **Propagated**: S7 `inst-sg-surface` + `inst-fg-trailing` + DoD + AC; S5 `inst-mat-registered`; PRD `fr-sellability-gate`; subscriptions SEAMS SUB-P5 (the adopted gate wording — owed with the D-80 horizon they already carry).

#### D-95 [M] Required-add-on coverage is per `(currency, region)`, not per currency

- **Where**: [`design/04-currency-tax.md`](./design/04-currency-tax.md) `inst-cb-addon` + DoD + AC; [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-invoice-currency-binding` (i).
- **Problem** (2026-07-31 slice review, M-7): case (i) required a **required** add-on to have "a row in a currency the base plan publishes" — the region axis unchecked, while S2's override-home rule demands the full pair for override targets. A required add-on covering EUR only in `US` while the base sells EUR in `EU` passed publish and died at order assembly — a market-shaped D-84 asymmetry one level up, discovered at order time instead of publish.
- **Decision**: **(review fix), 2026-07-31** — case (i) evaluates per **`(currency, region)`**: a required add-on — and an override target, now one rule with S2 `inst-cmp-override-home` — MUST have a covering published row for every `(currency, region)` pair the base plan sells; optional add-ons unchanged (attachment-time enforcement, D-73).
- **Propagated**: S4 `inst-cb-addon` + DoD + AC; PRD `fr-invoice-currency-binding`.

#### D-96 [M] `invoiceGroupingKey` gets its design home

- **Where**: [`design/02-plan-definition.md`](./design/02-plan-definition.md) §6 `pricing_plan` columns + §1.8 produced list.
- **Problem** (2026-07-31 slice review, M-8): the PRD glossary defines `invoiceGroupingKey` as an optional Plan field consumed by Billing for line layout, and S4 references it twice as existing — but no slice persisted, validated, or projected it: implementers could not build it, Billing could not consume it.
- **Decision**: **(review fix), 2026-07-31** — `pricing_plan.invoice_grouping_key` (nullable string; NULL/empty = no grouping), a Slice-2 plan-shape column revision-scoped like the rest, projected into the read model; a layout hint only — no validation beyond shape, and it never overrides the single-currency-per-invoice invariant (the S4 wording stands).
- **Propagated**: S2 §6 + §1.8.

#### D-97 [M] An add-on price-override reference binds the row's canonical scope key, resolving through the supersession chain

- **Where**: [`design/02-plan-definition.md`](./design/02-plan-definition.md) `inst-cmp-override-home`; [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-addon-rules`.
- **Problem** (2026-07-31 slice review, M-9): the override ref froze a specific `priceId` at the base plan's publish; a routine supersession of that row on the add-on's own plan left every referencing base plan pointing at a superseded row whose window closes at the changeover — retire is guarded (`RETIRE_PLAN_REFERENCED`), supersession was not, and whether the successor inherits the override role was unstated (read-time drift, the D-54 class).
- **Decision**: **(review fix), 2026-07-31** — the ref is authored as a `priceId` but **binds that row's canonical scope key**: resolution at `t` follows the key through windows exactly as any row resolves, so a supersession's successor legitimately serves the override (supersession is transparent to consumers by system doctrine, and the D-82/D-98 unit guard keeps the key's meaning stable); the base plan's publish validation checks the **key** is published and covers every sold `(currency, region)`. No guard on the superseding side is needed; coverage loss on the key is already governed by the window rules (D-62/D-80).
- **Propagated**: S2 `inst-cmp-override-home`; PRD `fr-addon-rules`. **Amended by D-116 (2026-07-31)**: the binding is the key **family modulo the market axes** — a single canonical scope key fixes one `(currency, region)` and therefore cannot itself cover every market the same rule requires; the subscriber's bound market selects the member key.

#### D-98 [M] `model_kind` joins the supersession-preserved field set on usage rows (D-82 amendment)

- **Where**: [`design/03-price-structure.md`](./design/03-price-structure.md) `inst-tb-supersession-units` + §5 code + DoD + fixture registry; [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §4.3; [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-supersession`.
- **Problem** (2026-07-31 slice review, L-6 escalated): D-82 preserved the unit/counter fields but not `model_kind`; a `graduated → volume`/`package` supersession mid-window re-prices the already-accumulated window total under new math (Variant A applies the selected band's single rate to the **whole** window `Q`, including units already rated marginally under the predecessor; package re-folds the window into blocks) — delta semantics no fixture named and no rule constrained.
- **Decision**: **(review fix), 2026-07-31 — flagged for veto** (it narrows the authorable surface): on a **usage-row** supersession the successor MUST also preserve `model_kind`; a kind change is a structural change and routes through plan revisioning + migration — the D-82 doctrine verbatim. `SUPERSESSION_UNIT_MISMATCH` covers the violation; the supersession-continuity fixture gains the negative kind-flip scenario. (`flat`↔`per_unit` on non-usage rows is unaffected — no counter exists there.)
- **Propagated**: S3 `inst-tb-supersession-units` + §5 + DoD + fixture note; S1 §4.3; PRD `fr-supersession`; the D-82 entry carries the amendment pointer.

#### D-99 [H] Window mutations are publish units

- **Where**: [`design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md`](./design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md) `inst-ws-publishunit` (new) + `inst-sg-surface` + §5 API table + DoD + AC + §10; [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §4.2 + §4.4; [PRD](./PRD.md) §17.5 increment table + `contract-tariffs-readmodel`.
- **Problem** (2026-07-31 slice review, H-1): the PRD's increment table already required a **window** edit to become addressable in a `CatalogVersion`, but the design requested addressability only on `PlanPublished`, D-06 extended publish units to overlays/memberships and stopped, and `subject_kind` had no window subject — so the standalone `WindowScheduler` surface requested nothing, warmed nothing, and emitted only `PriceWindow*` events, while the gate's predicate (1) and the D-80 coverage horizon are required to be evaluable from the **pinned** read model (`inst-sg-pinned`). A cancellation left the last-warmed delta advertising coverage the truth side had removed — selling into precisely the trailing void D-62 → D-80 → D-94 close, with `inst-fg-when`'s "no side door" true of the table and false of what consumers read; a coverage extension could not lift a horizon block until an unrelated publish happened to re-project the plan; and rating resolved a window state already changed.
- **Options**: (a) every window mutation is a publish unit re-projecting the plan subject (the D-06 treatment); (b) move the window/coverage predicates out of the pinned read model onto a live surface.
- **Decision**: **(a), 2026-07-31** — schedule / future-`effectiveTo` adjustment / cancellation each run validation → pending `CatalogVersion` ref → warm and re-project the affected rows' **plan** subject; windows are plan facts, so no new `subject_kind`. (b) rejected: it contradicts `inst-sg-pinned` and puts a live query on the order-time p95 < 100ms path. Paired clarification, which is what keeps the cost bounded: the read model carries window **intervals + states + coverage end**, never a point-in-time boolean, so **activation and expiry are not publish units** and need no re-projection — a frozen version never mutates and "active at `t`" is derived at read time. Cost: 3 projections per cutover, 2 per supersession, 2N per repricing run, all coalesced by the D-47 batching (bulk already coalesces per O5, so it is one delta row per affected plan per batch).
- **Propagated**: S7 ×6 sites; S1 §4.2 + §4.4; PRD §17.5 + `contract-tariffs-readmodel`.

#### D-100 [H] The cutover flips its predecessor — the second sanctioned `published → superseded` producer

- **Where**: [`design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md`](./design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md) `inst-co-supersede` (new) + `inst-gc-commit` + DoD + AC; [`design/03-price-structure.md`](./design/03-price-structure.md) `inst-ps-supersede`; [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §4.3.
- **Problem** (2026-07-31 slice review, H-2): the cutover's `all_subscriptions` successor lands on the **same** canonical scope key as the shortened predecessor — only the grandfathered copy moves to a new `cohort` key — and the Foundation admits one published row per key (partial `UNIQUE`). `inst-gc-commit` enumerated the entire transaction without the flip, while `inst-ps-supersede` and `SupersessionOrchestrator` both called the D-88 supersession unit *"the only path"* to it (*"there is no primitive-by-primitive path to this flip"*). Only S1's trigger column whitelist remembered the case ("on supersession/**cutover**"). Built from the documents the cutover commit inserts a second published row on an occupied key and dies on the unique index — the atomic unit fails wholesale and ADR-0002's mechanism never runs. The D-88 defect class with the operands swapped: the unit was defined and its multi-row sibling left holding a rule forbidding what the sibling must do.
- **Decision**: **2026-07-31** — the cutover commit flips the predecessor `published → superseded` inside its one ACID transaction, before inserting the successor; the cutover is named as the **second** sanctioned producer of the transition in the price-row state machine and the Foundation §4.3 taxonomy. The superseded predecessor stays live-resolvable until the changeover through its shortened window, per the standard flip-at-commit semantics. (Composing the cutover's `all_subscriptions` half *as* a supersession unit was considered; keeping one flip rule and two producers is the smaller change and leaves the cutover's per-key generation logic intact.)
- **Propagated**: S7 `inst-co-supersede` + `inst-gc-commit` + `dod-grandfathering` + AC; S3 `inst-ps-supersede`; S1 §4.3 ×2.

#### D-101 [H] Pin-eligibility is version-level (D-91 amendment)

- **Where**: [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §1.2 driver row + §3.6 + §4.4; [`design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md`](./design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md) `inst-ws-changeover-warm` (new) + §7 alarms + AC; [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-consumer-readmodel-resolution` + `contract-tariffs-readmodel`.
- **Problem** (2026-07-31 slice review, H-3): D-91 moved the warm marker to the subject row and blessed per-subject fallback (*"each falling back to its own prior completed delta meanwhile"*). A single pin then resolved two different contents over time — plan `P` at `V-3` before its delta warmed and at `V` after, with no version mutating — so a rating run pinning `V` could charge the pre-change price for one plan and the post-change price for another, and a replay of that pinned run produced different money. "Newest completed version", the referent of the ≤ 5s pin-lag rule, had also become undefined (it was a version-level property before D-91). The exposure is not the 5s SLO: a degraded warm re-drive *"continues past the SLO"* with no bound, and S1 §1.2 states the batching SLO governs the pre-commit delay "not … degraded handling". At a changeover the truth side shortens the predecessor and activates the successor on wall clock alone, so an un-warmed pin resolved the predecessor **with its pre-shorten open-ended interval** — the old price charged silently past the changeover, not fail-closed.
- **Options**: (a) version-level pin-eligibility (pinnable only when every subject of the version is warm), resolution still per subject; (b) per-subject pin with fail-closed on a subject that has not warmed at the pin.
- **Decision**: **(a), 2026-07-31** — a version is **pin-eligible** only once `CatalogVersionPublished` has fired and **every** subject row it projects is warm-complete; the ≤ 5s rule binds pin-eligible versions. Under it the per-subject fallback is **never observable at a pinned version** (every subject the version touched is warm at it; untouched subjects resolve their own frozen older deltas), so D-91's storage and its independent-warm parallelism survive untouched — only the pinning rule changes. (b) rejected: it fails closed for every consumer during any warm lag and contradicts the pin-lag rule. Un-pin-eligible past the batching SLO → Critical `pricing.readmodel.pin_eligibility_overdue` on the existing degraded path, consumers resolving the previous pin-eligible version **coherently** (uniformly pre-change) rather than a mixed set; a changeover reached in that state → Critical `pricing.window.changeover_unwarmed`, since the instant floors bound only the batching delay by construction.
- **Propagated**: S1 ×3 sites; S7 `inst-ws-changeover-warm` + §7 + AC; PRD ×2 sites; the D-91 entry carries the amendment pointer. **Amended by D-114 (2026-07-31)**: pin-eligibility is additionally **prefix-closed** (every earlier version pin-eligible) — per-subject resolution reaches through the pin into older deltas, so without the prefix a stuck older version's late warm made one pin resolve two contents over time.

#### D-102 [H] The `migrated-origin` read surface — and the one named exception to Tariffs-composes-the-snapshot

- **Where**: [`design/11-lifecycle.md`](./design/11-lifecycle.md) `inst-sy-surface` (new) + §1.7 + §5 + DoD + AC; [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §4.4; [`design/05-governance.md`](./design/05-governance.md) endpoint map; [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-migration-safety` + `contract-tariffs-readmodel`.
- **Problem** (2026-07-31 slice review, H-4): D-87 made the snapshot self-contained *so rating could charge from it*, and the PRD says *"Rating/Tariffs evaluate **from that payload**"* while `inst-sy-firstrating` has rating "retry against the frozen result" — but the payload sits in `pricing_snapshot_provenance`, deliberately outside the read model (S1 §4.4 names it "the one deliberately non-version-pinned reference"), and **nothing exposed it**: no S11 endpoint, nothing in S5's endpoint map (which claims to cover every REST surface of Slices 2–12), and none of the five §9.2 contracts. Compounding it, this is the one place the catalog composes a **per-subscription** snapshot, which D-30 (*"no per-subscription snapshot participation"*) and S9's DoD (*"the catalog never stamps snapshots"*) both forbid. The D-01/D-87 class one level out: the payload exists, the consumption path does not.
- **Decision**: **2026-07-31** — the surface is **`GET /bss-pricing/v1/migrated-origin-snapshots/{subscriptionRef}`**, `plan × read`, called by the Rating/Tariffs **service identity**, returning the frozen payload + provenance and 404 before synthesis (rating's fail-closed exception path); registered in the S5 endpoint map and as a second inbound lane of `contract-tariffs-readmodel`. Ownership is stated rather than left contradictory: D-30's denial is about the **customer-group resolution** case it was decided on; `migrated-origin` is the single named exception and is exceptional for a structural reason — there is no live plan state and no `CatalogVersion` for Tariffs to compose from, only governed history whose store has exactly one reader (D-76). The catalog therefore composes **only** this ref, serves it read-only, and composes no other per-subscription snapshot.
- **Propagated**: S11 ×5 sites; S1 §4.4; S5 endpoint map; PRD ×2 sites.

#### D-103 [M] Meter injectivity is per `(meter, dimensionKey)` line, not one meter per plan

- **Where**: [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-meter-injective` + the §17 Meter-injective row; [`design/02-plan-definition.md`](./design/02-plan-definition.md) `inst-cmp-injective` + `dod-composition` + AC.
- **Problem** (2026-07-31 slice review, M-1): `fr-meter-injective` said *"Each usage plan revision MUST map **exactly one** `meteringUnit`… Multi-meter offerings MUST be modeled as a derived (composite) meter or separate single-meter SKUs"*, and three rules of the same set assume the opposite: D-84's per-market completeness ranges over *"every `(meter, dimensionKey)` line the plan prices"*; S2's own D-84 integration AC exercises a plan pricing M1 and M2 (a plan `METER_AMBIGUOUS` already rejects, so the AC was unreachable and `USAGE_MARKET_INCOMPLETE` never fired for the stated reason); D-43's grant `applicability` scopes to *"an explicit **set** of published `meteringUnit` ids … usage lines of the grant-bearing plan"*, a set that could only ever hold one element. Two validators contradicted, and the resolution changes what each rule means.
- **Options**: (a) the invariant is per-`(meter, dimensionKey)` **line** per scope-key slice, and a plan MAY price several meters; (b) one meter per plan, with D-84 rescoped to dimension keys and `GRANT_APPLICABILITY_*` reduced to a single-element set.
- **Decision**: **(a), 2026-07-31 — CONFIRMED by the product owner 2026-07-31** (flagged because it relaxes an FR's literal MUST, so the authorable surface changes on paper). Decisive: the **enforcing** partial `UNIQUE` carries both `meter` and `dimension_key` and therefore always implemented (a) — the prose was the only place (b) existed; and the launch products argue the same way (a PaaS plan pricing cloudlets, storage and egress is one plan, not three, while a composite meter exists to price several *units as one billable line*: vCPU + RAM → one output unit, which (b) conflated with multi-meter pricing). A duplicate line within one slice remains the ambiguity that fails publish (`METER_AMBIGUOUS`); separate single-meter SKUs composed via bundle/add-ons remain available, just no longer mandatory.
- **Propagated**: PRD ×2 sites; S2 `inst-cmp-injective` + `dod-composition` + AC ×2.

#### D-104 [M] Bundle composition and rev-share changes are always material

- **Where**: [`design/08-bundles.md`](./design/08-bundles.md) `inst-ba-material` (new) + `dod-bundle-composition` + AC; [`design/05-governance.md`](./design/05-governance.md) `inst-mat-registered` + `dod-threshold` + AC; [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-bundle-composition`.
- **Problem** (2026-07-31 slice review, M-2): the `MaterialityEvaluator` computes per-row deltas over **price rows**, and `inst-mat-registered` listed no bundle trigger. A `sum_of_parts` component swap, a rev-share re-split, a `price_basis` or `invoiceItemization` change produces **no** price-row delta, so with a threshold configured each evaluated `auto_publishable` and reached consumers with **no approver** — while a $1 price-row change above threshold took two people. That is verbatim the hole D-50 closed for overlay lines, at comparable stakes: a rev-share split *is* vendor payout, and a component swap changes what the customer receives at an unchanged price. It also voided D-11's own justification for dropping `bundle × write` from the publish endpoint (*"the composition is protected at publish time by the approval content pin"*), because a non-material publish opens no approval record and therefore no pin.
- **Decision**: **2026-07-31 — CONFIRMED by the product owner 2026-07-31** (flagged because it adds an approval requirement to an operator workflow, the D-50 precedent's own flavor): bundle creation, component add/remove/replace, any rev-share change (`share_bp`, `platform_cut_bp`, `residual_absorber_party`), `price_basis` and `invoiceItemization` changes are **registered always-material triggers** — an overlay-line-shaped no-delta case, so the G1 fail-safe applies wholesale. `FinanceReviewer` already holds `bundle × read`, so the D-61 reviewability invariant needs no new grant.
- **Propagated**: S8 `inst-ba-material` + DoD + AC; S5 `inst-mat-registered` + DoD + AC; PRD `fr-bundle-composition`.

#### D-105 [M] Child-table keys carry their row discriminators; `pricing_bundle` gains `plan_id`

- **Where**: [`design/02-plan-definition.md`](./design/02-plan-definition.md) §6 `pricing_plan_addon_rule` + AC; [`design/08-bundles.md`](./design/08-bundles.md) §6 ×4 + AC.
- **Problem** (2026-07-31 slice review, M-3): D-83/D-92's "keyed `(X, plan_revision)`" phrasing dropped the discriminator on every 1:N child table except phases. `pricing_plan_addon_rule` was *"keyed by `(plan_id, plan_revision)`"* while holding one row per `addon_sku_id`; `pricing_bundle_component` one per component; `pricing_bundle_revshare` one per `(vendor_sku_id, party)`; `pricing_bundle_revshare_group` stated *"one row per `vendor_sku_id` within a revision"* in prose and omitted it from the key. Under those keys the `depends_on` cycle walk has one edge, "every referenced component" is one component, and "sum to 100% **per** included vendor SKU" is unsatisfiable. Separately `pricing_bundle` declared no `plan_id`, so D-92's *"a bundle rides its plan's revisions"* had no join path.
- **Decision**: **2026-07-31** — PKs become `(plan_id, plan_revision, addon_sku_id)`, `(bundle_id, plan_revision, component_plan_id)`, `(bundle_id, plan_revision, vendor_sku_id, party)` and `(bundle_id, plan_revision, vendor_sku_id)`; `pricing_bundle` gains the `plan_id` FK the revision keying presupposes. `pricing_plan_phase`'s `(phase_id, plan_revision)` was already the correct shape and is the pattern; `pricing_plan_descriptor_set` is genuinely 1:1 per revision and needs none.
- **Propagated**: S2 §6 + AC; S8 §6 ×4 + AC.

#### D-106 [M] Revision discipline reaches the last two plan child tables (D-83/D-92 completion)

- **Where**: [`design/10-advanced-primitives.md`](./design/10-advanced-primitives.md) §6 `pricing_plan_grant` / `pricing_grant_price` / `pricing_composite_meter` + AC.
- **Problem** (2026-07-31 slice review, M-4): D-83 versioned S2's three child tables, D-92 reached S8's and S9's, and the two remaining plan children were never swept. `pricing_plan_grant` (PK `grant_id`) and `pricing_composite_meter` (PK `composite_id`, plus an unexplained bare `revision` column) carried no `plan_revision` while holding structural, snapshot-frozen content — the grant's `category`/`applicability`/`drawdownPriority`/prices, and the composite formula whose own constraint A4 already claimed it was *"versioned with the plan revision"*. So a draft revision editing a grant's applicability or a composite formula mutated **published** truth, and a degraded-warm re-drive could leak the draft into a frozen version: the D-83 defect verbatim, third occurrence.
- **Decision**: **2026-07-31** — both version with the revision, **copy-on-new-revision**, with the identity half **stable across revisions** (`grant_id`, `composite_id` never re-minted — the `pricing_plan_phase` shape, chosen so the snapshot's grant reference and Billing's drawdown identity do not churn on an unrelated revision); `pricing_grant_price` is re-keyed `(grant_id, plan_revision, currency, region)`; the bare `revision` column is replaced by `plan_revision`. Published rows are immutable with their revision and are the projection source for warm and re-drive alike.
- **Propagated**: S10 §6 ×3 tables + AC.

#### D-107 [M] Overlay uniqueness and interval-overlap checks are revision-scoped

- **Where**: [`design/09-price-overlays.md`](./design/09-price-overlays.md) §6 `precedence` column + `inst-plv-dating` + AC.
- **Problem** (2026-07-31 slice review, M-5): D-92 keyed `pricing_price_overlay` `(price_overlay_id, revision)` with `draft | published | superseded` rows coexisting, and left `UNIQUE (tenant_id, scope_class, precedence)` unqualified — so opening a draft revision of a published overlay collides with **itself** and every edit of a live overlay fails `PRECEDENCE_DUPLICATE`. The same applies to `OVERLAY_INTERVAL_OVERLAP`, whose collision key would always match the overlay's own published revision. The price rows and plan revisions both got the partial `WHERE lifecycle_state = 'published'` treatment (D-90 leans on it explicitly); the overlay checks did not travel with the decision that created the revisions.
- **Decision**: **2026-07-31** — the precedence index becomes partial on `lifecycle_state = 'published'`, and the interval-overlap check evaluates published revisions only and never against another revision of the same `price_overlay_id` (the collision domain is *other* overlays).
- **Propagated**: S9 §6 + `inst-plv-dating` + AC.

#### D-108 [M] The contract lock is structural; price movement is Contracts-owned

- **Where**: [`design/11-lifecycle.md`](./design/11-lifecycle.md) `inst-cl-scope` (new) + `dod-contract-lock` + AC; [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-contract-locked-protection`, `fr-plan-retirement`.
- **Problem** (2026-07-31 slice review, M-6): both the FR and `inst-cl-reject` read *"while an active contract references a Plan **revision**, structural plan mutation MUST be rejected, directing the operator to a new revision or contract expiry"* — unenforceable in both directions. A published revision row is **immutable in content** by construction (§4.3), so the guard named an operation that cannot occur; and D-56 deliberately kept `pricing_price` on `plan_id`, **not** on a revision, so locking a revision protected no price. Every mutation that actually moves a locked subscription's economics at renewal — a supersession or repricing run on the plan's keys, a cutover, an overlay line targeting the plan — sat outside the guard, unenumerated. D-78 established that "the row is immutable and the effective charge moved anyway" is a real defect class.
- **Options**: (a) enumerate a price-side block (locked ⇒ supersession/cutover/overlay on the plan's keys reject); (b) declare the lock **structural** and state that the negotiated rate is Contracts-owned.
- **Decision**: **(b), 2026-07-31 — CONFIRMED by the product owner 2026-07-31** (flagged because it states normatively that repricing does not break a lock, a commercial statement). While an active contract references revision `R`: a **new** revision MUST NOT drop or re-shape what `R` exposes to the contract's subscriptions (the priced `(meter, dimensionKey)` line set, the phase chain, the required add-on set, the descriptor set), and the plan MUST NOT be **retired** (`CONTRACT_LOCKED`, 409, referencing contracts enumerated) — the guard the FR's own rationale can actually enforce, composing with `inst-ph-terminal-stable`/`PHASE_IN_USE` rather than duplicating them. **Price movement is explicitly permitted**: the negotiated rate is Contracts-owned (PRD §13; S10 leaves negotiated RI rates to Contracts) and resolves ahead of the catalog rate, the frozen snapshot covers the in-flight period, and (a) would let one contracted account freeze an entire market's annual reprice. Stating the permission normatively is the point — so nobody builds the block, and so the asymmetry with D-78 (where the catalog *had* to act, because an overlay moves the catalog's **own** effective charge) is deliberate.
- **Propagated**: S11 `inst-cl-scope` + `dod-contract-lock` + AC; PRD `fr-contract-locked-protection` + `fr-plan-retirement`.

#### D-109 [M] Plan retirement is always material, unconditionally

- **Where**: [`design/11-lifecycle.md`](./design/11-lifecycle.md) `inst-re-governed` + `dod-retirement` + AC; [`design/05-governance.md`](./design/05-governance.md) `inst-mat-registered` + `dod-threshold` + AC; [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-plan-retirement`.
- **Problem** (2026-07-31 slice review, M-7): D-62 made a single window cancel/shorten always-material precisely because one operator could otherwise *"silently revert a two-person-approved price change"* — yet retirement cancels **every** not-yet-active window on its zero-subscriber keys in one call under `plan × retire`, and `inst-mat-registered` registered it only for the D-05 case (a live cutover unit to unwind). It also stops all new sales for the plan and is **irreversible**: no `retired → published` edge (`inst-pl-norollback`) and the open draft revision is deleted with it. The only control was a dry-run confirm screen.
- **Decision**: **2026-07-31 — CONFIRMED by the product owner 2026-07-31** (flagged as operational-control flavored, the D-34 precedent): retirement is a registered always-material trigger in **every** case. A confirm screen is not a second principal, and the approving `FinanceReviewer` already holds `plan × read`, so the D-61 reviewability invariant is satisfied without a new grant.
- **Propagated**: S11 `inst-re-governed` + DoD + AC; S5 `inst-mat-registered` + DoD + AC; PRD `fr-plan-retirement`.

#### D-110 [M] `taxCategory` rides the price row; one display basis per market

- **Where**: [`design/04-currency-tax.md`](./design/04-currency-tax.md) §6 + `inst-td-basis-uniform` (new) + §5 code + DoD + AC; [`design/02-plan-definition.md`](./design/02-plan-definition.md) §1.6 P5 + `inst-ds-required` + §6 + `dod-descriptors`; [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-tax-display-basis`, `fr-billing-descriptors`, `contract-billing-descriptors`, AC #53.
- **Problem** (2026-07-31 slice review, M-8): S4 declared `tax_category_ref` the **source of truth** for a **row's** tax category and then said *"the D-48 billing-descriptor set's tax-category field mirrors it, with a publish-time consistency check — a mismatch fails publish"* — but `pricing_plan_descriptor_set` holds exactly one `tax_category` per `(plan_id, plan_revision)`, while a plan legitimately carries different categories per row (subscription vs data transfer) and per region. The check was undefined the moment two rows differed and, read literally, forced one category per plan — which no rule states and which the per-row column exists to avoid. Adjacent and unstated: nothing constrained `tax_inclusive` uniformity across the keys of one `(currency, region)`.
- **Decision**: **2026-07-31 — CONFIRMED by the product owner 2026-07-31, both halves** (flagged because the second half narrows the authorable surface). (1) The descriptor set's `tax_category` column is **removed** and the mirror/consistency rule with it: `taxCategory` **rides the price row** as `tax_category_ref`, exactly the treatment `billingTiming` received in D-48's own 2026-07-28 amendment — so the v1 contract's **content** is unchanged (three descriptor-set fields + two row-borne elements) and Billing's pending countersign covers the shape. (2) Every published row of a plan on one `(currency, region)` MUST carry the same `tax_inclusive` value (`TAX_BASIS_MIXED_MARKET`, 422): an invoice is one document and the line template has no per-line basis to switch on. The symptom of (2)'s absence was misdirected rather than absent — under the D-94 conjunction one tax-inclusive component key flags its market `not_sellable_ga` and the whole plan-market silently becomes unsellable with no publish-time explanation; post-Tax-Engine-GA the same authoring becomes a mixed-basis invoice. Per market, not per plan: tax-exclusive US + tax-inclusive EU stays legal.
- **Propagated**: S4 ×5 sites; S2 ×4 sites; PRD ×4 sites.

#### D-111 [M] What a bulk run's per-row commit re-validates

- **Where**: [`design/12-operator-efficiency.md`](./design/12-operator-efficiency.md) `inst-mr-validate-scope` (new) + `dod-mass-repricing` + §10 + AC.
- **Problem** (2026-07-31 slice review, M-9, performance): `inst-su-commit` has the supersession unit's commit *"re-run the pipeline"* and `inst-mr-apply` has *"each applied row … a supersession unit … executed inside the per-row transaction"*. The pipeline is the **aggregate** rule set — D-21 puts window/phase coverage, hybrid completeness, meter injectivity and the fixture gate at publish only — so a run over N rows of one plan performs N whole-plan validations inside row-level transactions, i.e. O(rows × plan-validation cost), against a ratified ≥ 50 rows/s. S12 §10 sized only the window writes; the pipeline re-run arrived with D-88, after the figure was perf-verified.
- **Decision**: **2026-07-31** — in a bulk run the **per-row** commit re-runs the **row-local** rule set (D-21's save-time set + the D-82/D-98 unit guard) plus **the touched key's** window overlap/gap/trailing-void check — the checks whose inputs the row itself changes — while the **plan-level aggregate** pass runs **once per plan per run** inside the approval-to-commit boundary over the run's frozen row set. Sound because the aggregate checks evaluate identical content for every row of a plan; safe because the row set is frozen at selector expansion and interactive edits on contained keys are already blocked (`inst-mp-pending`, the bulk lock). A plan whose aggregate pass fails marks **all** its rows `failed` with the shared reason — never a partial plan. The perf test MUST exercise both the D-88 window operations and this split. **Amended by D-124 (2026-08-01)**: the pass runs at **entry to `committing`** (the justification above cited `inst-mp-pending` and the bulk lock for a window neither covers — the pin is selector-time-only and the lock starts at `committing`) and evaluates the **plan's full post-run row set** (the run's frozen subset cannot decide plan-level rules for the plan's untouched markets).
- **Propagated**: S12 `inst-mr-validate-scope` + DoD + §10 + AC ×2.

#### D-112 [M] An `overlay_index` subject gives overlay evaluation an access path

- **Where**: [`design/09-price-overlays.md`](./design/09-price-overlays.md) §7 + §10 + AC; [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §3.7 `subject_kind`.
- **Problem** (2026-07-31 slice review, M-10, performance): D-91 projects one row per overlay subject and forbids re-projecting targeted plans (*"Tariffs joins overlays to base rows at evaluation"*) — but evaluation needs the **set** of scope-matching overlays live at the pin, and the delta store is keyed and indexed by subject, so no path existed from a plan / brand / region / customer group to that set. The only route the documents left was a `DISTINCT subject_ref` scan across the retained overlay deltas (retention = the truth-history horizon, ≥ 7y) plus a greatest-≤-pin probe per subject, on the order-time path with a p95 < 100ms budget — the D-86 explosion avoided on the write side and reintroduced on the read side. S9 §10 sized membership resolution only.
- **Decision**: **2026-07-31** — each overlay publish unit **also** re-projects one tenant-scoped **`subject_kind = overlay_index`** row: the live overlay id set with each overlay's `(scope_class, scope_value, effective interval, precedence)`. Enumeration becomes one indexed read at the pin plus one document read per scope match; the index costs one extra delta row per overlay commit (not one per targeted plan), so D-91's "one row per publish unit" property becomes two and stays O(publish units). Membership needs no analogue — it resolves by `subject_ref = payer` directly.
- **Propagated**: S9 §7 + §10 + AC; S1 §3.7 + §4.4.

#### D-113 [H] The plan-change carry-vs-reset flag gets a pricing home — it existed nowhere while two gears consumed it "from the pinned snapshot"

- **Where**: [`design/06-consumer-contracts.md`](./design/06-consumer-contracts.md) `inst-pc-counter-carry` (new) + §6 + DoD + AC; [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-plan-change-contract` + `contract-tariffs-readmodel`; counterpart surfaces: rating `design/09` §Frozen-inputs, `design/03` §4.3, `design/13`, T-D-12, rating PRD §1.4/§6.11/§17.2; subscriptions PRD §plan-change.
- **Problem** (2026-07-31c slice review, H-1): rating's plan-change semantics hang on a snapshot-frozen flag pricing never defines. Rating `design/09` names the owner explicitly — *"Pricing (Product Catalog) — … **carry-vs-reset flags — all in the pinned snapshot**"*, citing pricing `design/06` — T-D-12 routes *"plan-change per carry-vs-reset flag"*, rating PRD §17.2 reads *"default reset unless marked carry"*, and subscriptions PRD restates the dependency. Pricing S6 publishes no such field: no FR, no column, no validation, no projection, no §9.2 line — and no SEAMS row tracks the gap (P1/P2 are the `prorationBasis` enum and the anchor clamp). Rating's own two absence readings even disagree (`design/09`: *"all fail-closed when absent"*; rating PRD: default reset) — two implementations of one seam diverge on money. The D-01/D-65/D-79/D-87 class, for the first time **between gears**.
- **Decision**: **2026-07-31 — flagged for veto · joint with Rating + Subscriptions.** S6 authors **`usage_counter_on_plan_change ∈ {reset (default) | carry}`** — a plan column, revision-scoped, projected and snapshot-frozen like its S6 siblings. At an in-place plan-change boundary rating slice 09 routes the **target** plan's frozen flag (the plan whose bands consume the continued `Q` accepts the continuity liability). **`carry` is honoured per shared `(meter, dimensionKey)` line and only where the D-82/D-98 unit field set (incl. `package_size` per D-122) matches between the source and target rows across both frozen snapshots** — a mismatched line **resets** (+ a rating-side operator signal), never carries a counter across denominations: the counter key is plan-blind, publish-time guarding is impossible across independent plans, and an unguarded `carry` is the ×24 class through its fourth door (D-82 closed supersession, D-98 the kind flip, D-89 the phase axis). **Absence default = `reset`** (the rating-PRD reading wins; a missing flag on an old snapshot is a reset, never a rating failure — rating `design/09`'s "fail-closed when absent" is reconciled to this). Commitment-**pool** carry stays rating/Contracts-side pending the deferred committed-usage authoring — named explicitly so that gap is not recreated silently: pricing publishes no pool flag, and rating's pool default stays reset. **Owed adoptions**: Rating — consume from the target snapshot, per-line unit check, absence default, a SEAMS row for the field; Subscriptions — informational (the WHEN/MATH split is unchanged).
- **Propagated**: S6 `inst-pc-counter-carry` + §6 column + `dod-plan-change` + AC; PRD `fr-plan-change-contract` + `contract-tariffs-readmodel`.

#### D-114 [H] Pin-eligibility is prefix-closed (D-101 amendment)

- **Where**: [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §1.2 driver row + §3.6 + §4.4; [`design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md`](./design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md) AC; [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-consumer-readmodel-resolution` + `contract-tariffs-readmodel`.
- **Problem** (2026-07-31c slice review, H-2): D-101 defined pin-eligibility over the version's **own** subjects while resolution stays *"greatest completed version ≤ the pin"* — reaching **through** the pin into older deltas that nothing requires to be complete. Warms are per-subject and parallel, and a degraded re-drive *"continues past the SLO"* unbounded. So with `V5` degraded (plan `A` unwarm) and `V6` (plan `B`) fully warm, `V6` is pin-eligible by the letter of D-101; a consumer pinning `V6` resolves `A@V4` — and after `V5`'s re-drive completes, the **same pin** resolves `A@V5`. §4.4's supporting claim (*"untouched subjects resolve their own older deltas, which are frozen and never change"*) is false exactly here: the older delta existed-but-unwarm and completed later. One pin, two contents, replayed money differs — the defect D-101 was raised to close, one version out.
- **Decision**: **2026-07-31** — a version is pin-eligible only once `CatalogVersionPublished` has fired, every subject row it projects is warm-complete, **and every earlier version is itself pin-eligible**: pin-eligibility is a **monotonic frontier**. D-91's per-subject storage and parallel warms survive untouched; "the newest pin-eligible version" is the frontier edge, so the ≤ 5s pin-lag rule and "consumers keep pinning the previous pin-eligible version" regain exact referents, and the frozen-older-deltas claim becomes true by construction. The cost is honest and already instrumented: a stuck version holds the frontier, which is precisely what `pricing.readmodel.pin_eligibility_overdue` (Critical) signals.
- **Propagated**: S1 §1.2 + §3.6 + §4.4; S7 AC (the D-101 scenario gains the stuck-older-version leg); PRD ×2 sites; the D-101 entry carries the amendment pointer.

#### D-115 [H] The materiality delta domain — and the no-computable-delta rule

- **Where**: [`design/05-governance.md`](./design/05-governance.md) `inst-mat-percurrency` + `inst-mat-registered` + `dod-threshold` + AC; [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-approval-threshold-policy`.
- **Problem** (2026-07-31c slice review, H-3): the evaluator's delta was defined only as *"an absolute amount or percentage delta per currency"* per row. Three legs: (a) for `graduated`/`volume`/`package` rows `amount_minor` is NULL by construction (`AMOUNT_PLACEMENT_INVALID`), so "the row's delta" had **no defined operand** for exactly the rows carrying tiered revenue, and a percent policy against a `$0` baseline divides by zero; (b) quantity-semantics fields move money at zero amount delta — `manual_quantity` (10 → 1000 seats = ×100 the charge), `package_size`, tier-band **bounds** (`[0,1000)` → `[0,10)`), `includedAllowance.quantity` — none in the D-82/D-98 preserved set (they are the legitimate *content* of a price change), all `auto_publishable` under a configured threshold: one person, no approval record, no content pin; (c) contract and shape fields have no numeric delta at all — `billingTiming`, `prorationBasis`, `billing_anchor_policy`, `credit_on_downgrade`, `tax_inclusive`/`tax_category_ref`, `quantity_source`; descriptor fields (GL code!), phase graph/durations (trial 7 → 90 days), the add-on rule set, cycle/frequency, availability dates, `PlanTier` override, `invoiceGroupingKey` — a pure-shape revision contains zero price rows, so nothing trips. The register closed this class four times (D-50 overlays, D-104 bundles — whose rationale applies verbatim — D-62 windows, D-109 retirement) and S10's grant non-price fields got the G1 treatment explicitly; these siblings never did.
- **Decision**: **2026-07-31 — flagged for veto** (governance-strictness flavor, the D-50/D-104 precedent). (1) **Delta domain defined**: `flat`/`per_unit` → the `amount_minor` delta; `graduated`/`volume` → the band-wise `unit_price_minor` vector compared per band **iff the band geometry (bounds and count) is unchanged**; `package` → the `package_price_minor` delta **iff `package_size` is unchanged**. (2) **Geometry/quantity changes are material**: any change to band bounds/count, `package_size`, `manual_quantity`, or `includedAllowance.quantity` — no effective-price delta is computable catalog-side (the no-charge-computation principle forbids computing one), so the G1 fail-safe applies; a percent-only policy against a zero baseline is likewise material. (3) **The no-computable-delta trigger, generalized**: a mutation whose change set carries no computable price delta — the row contract fields and plan-shape revision content enumerated above — is **always material** (registered trigger; the grant-field precedent applied to its siblings). Auto-publish remains exactly: a pure amount change on unchanged geometry, below an explicitly configured threshold, not a first publish. **Enumeration completed 2026-08-01** (billing-domain review, C-7): the plan-change contract content — `usage_counter_on_plan_change` (the same-wave D-113 lever this list predated), `allowed_change_targets`, `comparability_rank` — joins the registered plan-shape set; the blanket no-computable-delta clause already covered it on a plain reading, but the registered list is what implementers code from.
- **Propagated**: S5 `inst-mat-percurrency` + `inst-mat-registered` + `dod-threshold` + AC; PRD `fr-approval-threshold-policy`.

#### D-116 [M] The add-on override ref binds the scope-key family modulo market (D-97 amendment)

- **Where**: [`design/02-plan-definition.md`](./design/02-plan-definition.md) `inst-cmp-override-home`; [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-addon-rules`.
- **Problem** (2026-07-31c slice review, M-1): D-97 wrote *"the ref binds that row's canonical scope key"* and, in the same rule, *"covers every `(currency, region)` the base plan sells"* — unsatisfiable together, since a canonical scope key **fixes** one `(currency, region)`; resolution for a subscriber on any other market had no defined path (there was no key to follow through windows there).
- **Decision**: **2026-07-31** — the ref binds the referenced row's **key family modulo the market axes**: `(addon planId, priceOverlay = base, phase, priceEligibility = all_subscriptions, chargeKind, cohort = none)` with `(currency, region)` free. The subscriber's bound market selects the member key; resolution at `t` follows **that member** through windows (supersession-transparent exactly as D-97 intended); the D-95 coverage walk validates a published, covering member for every market the base plan sells.
- **Propagated**: S2 `inst-cmp-override-home`; PRD `fr-addon-rules`; the D-97 entry carries the amendment pointer.

#### D-117 [M] A phase-scoped usage override requires its terminal-phase base line (orphans forbidden)

- **Where**: [`design/02-plan-definition.md`](./design/02-plan-definition.md) `inst-ph-usage-invariant` + §5 code + `dod-phases` + AC; [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-plan-phases`.
- **Problem** (2026-07-31c slice review, M-2): `inst-ph-usage-invariant` defines the phase-scoped row as an **override** of the terminal-phase row but never requires that base to exist. For an orphan line (priced only on a non-terminal phase): the D-89 unit guard loses its comparison target, D-84's per-market completeness exempts it (*"phase-scoped overrides are additive and exempt"*), and after the phase converts the line resolves to **nothing** — usage that continues fails closed on a published, sellable plan. "Sold but unrateable" through the override door, after D-15/D-64/D-84 closed three others.
- **Decision**: **2026-07-31 — flagged for veto** (it removes an expressible shape: phase-limited-only metering). A phase-scoped usage row MUST have the phase-invariant terminal-phase row of its `(meter, dimensionKey)` line — `PHASE_OVERRIDE_ORPHANED` (422) otherwise. The D-53 posture: fail closed rather than ship undecided semantics; first-class phase-limited lines (own D-84 completeness, defined conversion-to-nothing) are a named Future gate if the shape is ever wanted.
- **Propagated**: S2 ×4 sites; PRD `fr-plan-phases`.

#### D-118 [M] Bulk import is draft-plane authoring; published-row changes route through repricing runs

- **Where**: [`design/12-operator-efficiency.md`](./design/12-operator-efficiency.md) `inst-bk-phase1` + flow + §5 code + `dod-bulk-import` + AC; [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-bulk-price-import`.
- **Problem** (2026-07-31c slice review, M-3): the import commits *"per-row under optimistic locks (ETag)"* — but a published row cannot be UPDATEd (append-only), and the only sanctioned change paths are the D-88 supersession unit and the cutover, both with window operations and a changeover-instant floor. Mass repricing was explicitly rebuilt on those (D-88/D-111); the import got neither an instant nor unit language, while its concurrency story only makes sense against mutable rows. As written, one team builds import-as-draft-authoring and another import-as-bulk-supersession missing the instant floor — incompatible, the second reopening the transient fail-closed window D-88 closed.
- **Decision**: **2026-07-31 — flagged for veto** (it narrows an operator workflow). Import rows land on the **draft plane**: new scope keys, or edits of existing draft rows under their ETags. A row addressing a **published** row's key with changed content fails Phase 1 **per-row** (`IMPORT_TARGETS_PUBLISHED`, remediation named: a repricing run — which carries the D-88 supersession units and the single bounded changeover instant). One bulk mechanism for published prices; D-88's floor stays universal; the ETag/bulk-lock story binds draft rows for imports and published rows for repricing runs.
- **Propagated**: S12 ×4 sites; PRD `fr-bulk-price-import`.

#### D-119 [M] One tax display basis per bundle-market (the D-110 rule across components)

- **Where**: [`design/08-bundles.md`](./design/08-bundles.md) `inst-bc-taxbasis` (new) + §5 code + `dod-bundle-composition` + AC; [`design/04-currency-tax.md`](./design/04-currency-tax.md) `inst-td-basis-uniform` cross-ref; [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-bundle-composition`.
- **Problem** (2026-07-31c slice review, M-4): D-110 pinned one display basis per `(currency, region)` **per plan** (*"an invoice is one document"*). A bundle composes several plans onto exactly one invoice: component A tax-inclusive + component B tax-exclusive in one market passes every S8 check (coverage checks currency/region/frequency only), masked pre-Tax-Engine only incidentally by the GA gate; the moment the Tax Engine GAs and A re-publishes clear, the bundle sells the mixed-basis invoice D-110 forbids within one plan — assembled across plans.
- **Decision**: **2026-07-31 — flagged for veto** (a new publish constraint on composition). Bundle publish validates that all component rows — and the bundle's own rows for `own_price` — on one sold `(currency, region)` carry the **same** `tax_inclusive` value (`BUNDLE_TAX_BASIS_MIXED`, 422, divergent components named). The reverse guard rides the D-54 pattern: a component re-publish that would mix a referencing bundle's market fails the same way, the referencing bundle enumerated — otherwise the bundle-side check is a point-in-time promise a later component publish silently breaks.
- **Propagated**: S8 ×4 sites; S4 cross-ref; PRD `fr-bundle-composition`.

#### D-120 [M] Overlay scope values validate against declared universes

- **Where**: [`design/09-price-overlays.md`](./design/09-price-overlays.md) `inst-plv-scope`; [`design/04-currency-tax.md`](./design/04-currency-tax.md) `inst-tx-brand` (generalized) + `inst-tx-mutation` + §6 + `dod-taxonomy` + §5 codes; [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-priceoverlay-authoring` + the §17.4 rule row.
- **Problem** (2026-07-31c slice review, M-5): `inst-plv-scope` validated scope values for `brand` and `customerGroup` only. A **region**-scoped overlay's value was never checked against `pricing_region_taxonomy`, and S4's retire guard enumerated price rows and brand overlays only — a region value retired cleanly while region-scoped overlays still named it. **partner** and **orgTier** had no declared value universe anywhere (the PRD defines the concepts, nothing defines legal values) — free-form strings on the axis that selects **who receives an adjustment**, the §F.2 `rounding_policy_ref` pattern — and no contract names the payer → partner/orgTier resolution input Tariffs matches against.
- **Decision**: **2026-07-31** — (i) region scope values validate against the region taxonomy (active values), and the S4 retire guard extends to **every** overlay scope referencing a taxonomy value (region/brand/partner/orgTier). (ii) partner and orgTier get **tenant-configured taxonomies** of the S4 shape (`pricing_partner_taxonomy`, `pricing_org_tier_taxonomy`; CatalogAdmin, `config × write`, audited, retire-guarded) as the MVP value universe — the **D-01 pattern**: tenant-declared now, reconciliation against the external SoR (AMS/partner registry) when one exists, a named Future joint item. (iii) The payer → `(partner, orgTier)` resolution input is registered as a **needs-decision** line on the Tariffs contract — who supplies the payer's partner standing at evaluation is currently stated nowhere.
- **Propagated**: S9 `inst-plv-scope`; S4 ×5 sites; PRD ×2 sites.

#### D-121 [M] The plan-subject projection's row/window set and horizon (D-86/D-99 completion)

- **Where**: [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §4.4 window-facts paragraph + §3.7 `pricing_read_model`; [PRD](./PRD.md) `contract-tariffs-readmodel`.
- **Problem** (2026-07-31c slice review, M-6): nothing defined which **rows** a plan-subject delta carries (lifecycle states; superseded-but-window-live predecessors; how far back window history reaches), and §4.4's only enumeration — *"intervals and states (`scheduled | active`)"* — **excludes `expired`**. Rating pins current versions and rates past instants (arrears always lag): after any changeover the predecessor's window is `expired`, so the first re-projection (any publish unit touching the plan) drops it from the delta, and resolution at yesterday's `t` under the new delta finds no covering window — a legitimately covered period fails closed. The inverse reading (project everything forever) is the perf hazard instead: every delta re-carries the plan's full accumulated row+window history, delta size grows with plan age, total storage superlinearly with publish count, at ≥ 7y retention.
- **Decision**: **2026-07-31** — a plan-subject delta projects **every price row of the plan (any lifecycle state except never-published drafts) whose window set intersects `[projection_time − H, ∞)`**, each with its full interval set and state (`scheduled | active | expired` — cancelled windows are not projected), where **`H` = 2 × the longest billing cycle sold on the plan** (floor: one cycle + the D-47 bulk SLO; tenant-configurable upward). That covers arrears/correction resolution on open periods at a current pin; anything older resolves by **replaying from the originally-pinned version** (deltas are retained on the truth horizon per D-86, so old pins stay resolvable; posted periods never re-query per `fr-pricing-snapshot`). Delta size is thereby bounded O(live keys × windows within `H`), never O(plan history).
- **Propagated**: S1 §4.4 + §3.7; PRD `contract-tariffs-readmodel`. **Completed by D-167** (2026-08-03): the horizon filters on a window set Slice 7 has not built, so the rule is unevaluable today and the premise under which the projection proceeds anyway — no producer for `published → superseded`, hence no accumulated history to exclude — is stated with it.

#### D-122 [M] `package_size` joins the supersession/phase-override preserved set (D-82/D-98/D-89 amendment)

- **Where**: [`design/03-price-structure.md`](./design/03-price-structure.md) `inst-tb-supersession-units` + §5 code + `dod-tier-bands` + §6 fixture note + AC; [`design/02-plan-definition.md`](./design/02-plan-definition.md) `inst-ph-override-units` + §5 code; [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §4.3; [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-supersession` + `fr-plan-phases`.
- **Problem** (2026-07-31c slice review, M-7): the preserved set guards `meter`/`dimensionKey`/`model_kind`/granularities/aggregation+qualification windows — not `package_size`. Block math is **non-linear in the window** (D-58's own argument) and rating counts blocks by **cumulative ceil-diff** (T-D-12), which presupposes one block size per window: a `package → package` supersession (or phase override) changing `package_size` 100 → 10 mid-window re-buckets every unit already consumed — the D-98 retroactivity inside one kind, and the ceil-diff attribution is ill-defined across the change. `package_price_minor` remains the legitimate price lever (the analogue of a band's `unit_price_minor`); the block **size** is quantity semantics (band bounds' analogue — those are covered by D-115's materiality, but bands are *incremental* math, which is why they stay legal on supersession while `package_size` cannot be).
- **Decision**: **2026-07-31 — flagged for veto** (the D-98 precedent's flavor: it removes a mid-window change capability, routing it through revisioning + migration). `package_size` joins the preserved field set on usage-row supersession (`SUPERSESSION_UNIT_MISMATCH`) and phase-scoped overrides (`PHASE_OVERRIDE_UNIT_MISMATCH`); the supersession-continuity fixture gains the negative `package_size`-change scenario.
- **Propagated**: S3 ×5 sites; S2 ×2 sites; S1 §4.3; PRD ×2 sites; the D-82 entry carries the amendment pointer.

#### D-123 [H] One proration/anchor contract per plan-market — uniformity across the recurring rows of one `(currency, region)`

- **Where**: [`design/06-consumer-contracts.md`](./design/06-consumer-contracts.md) `inst-pi-uniform` (new) + §5 code + §6 note + `dod-proration-inputs` + AC; [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-proration-input-contract` + AC #61; counterpart surfaces (informational): subscriptions `design/01-foundation-lifecycle` §Subscription aggregate (`billingAnchor`) + subscriptions PRD glossary/§plan-change (`prorationBasis` read as one value); rating `design/09` (per-`SubWindowSlice` snapshots).
- **Problem** (2026-07-31d billing-domain review, H-1): `billing_anchor_policy` is a **`pricing_price` column** and `inst-pi-required` makes all three proration fields mandatory on every recurring row; D-15 (`inst-ph-coverage`) requires recurring coverage of every phase per sold `(currency, region)` and phase is a scope-key axis (ADR-0002) — so a phased plan carries N recurring rows per market, each with its own anchor. The consuming side reads **one** value: subscriptions' `billingAnchor` is a single field on the Subscription aggregate ("the PRD's cycle-boundary rule") and subscriptions' PRD reads `prorationBasis` as one value "applied to all mid-period proration of the recurring component". No rule related the N authored values to the one consumed value: an intro-pricing plan (intro → evergreen, both charging) authoring `subscription_start` on the intro row and `fixed_day(1)` on the terminal row published both anchors into one frozen snapshot, and nothing said which sets the subscription's cycle boundary — one implementation keeps the original boundary at phase conversion, another shifts it to the 1st and prorates a partial period. Neither gear's documents mentioned anchor × phase interaction at all. Usage rows were deliberately made phase-invariant (`inst-ph-usage-invariant`) to avoid exactly this class; recurring rows got the opposite treatment with no coherence rule. `prorationBasis`/`creditOnDowngrade` are the same class one notch weaker (rating splits per `SubWindowSlice` "each with its own snapshot" and `inst-pi-credit-source` already names the governing row across a change — but within one market the value was still N-valued).
- **Decision**: **2026-08-01 — flagged for veto · joint with Subscriptions + Rating** (the D-110 shape: "an invoice is one document" → **a subscription is one cycle clock**; like D-110 it narrows the authorable surface, hence the flag). Every published recurring row of a plan on one `(currency, region)` MUST carry the **same** `billingAnchorPolicy` (incl. `anchor_day`), the same `prorationBasis` and the same `creditOnDowngrade` — a divergent market fails publish (`PRORATION_CONTRACT_MIXED_MARKET`, 422, divergent rows and fields named). Per market, not per plan (EU on the 1st + US on signup day stays legal). The phase axis is thereby cycle-clock-neutral **by construction**: phase conversion never moves the anchor-derived boundary and never changes proration math, and the single value Subscriptions reads is the market's uniform value — well-defined at any `t`, any phase; rating's per-slice snapshots agree across slices by construction. `billingTiming` is exempt (deliberately per-row: a hybrid mixes `in_advance` base + `in_arrears` usage and Billing consumes it per line). `inst-pi-credit-source` unchanged — it picks which **snapshot's** value governs across a plan change; this rule makes the pick unambiguous within one market. **Owed adoptions**: Subscriptions and Rating — informational only (their single-value readings become well-founded; no field or contract change).
- **Propagated**: S6 `inst-pi-uniform` + §5 + §6 + `dod-proration-inputs` + AC; PRD `fr-proration-input-contract` + AC #61.

#### D-124 [M] The bulk aggregate pass runs at commit entry, over the plan's full post-run row set (D-111 amendment)

- **Where**: [`design/12-operator-efficiency.md`](./design/12-operator-efficiency.md) `inst-mr-validate-scope` + `dod-mass-repricing` + §10 + AC ×2; the D-111 entry carries the amendment pointer.
- **Problem** (2026-07-31d billing-domain review, M-1 + M-2): D-111 hoisted the plan-level aggregate pass to run once per plan *"inside the run's approval-to-commit boundary"* over *"the run's frozen row set"*, justified by *"an interactive edit on a contained key is already blocked (`inst-mp-pending`, the bulk lock)"*. Both citations fail for that window: `inst-mp-pending` is a **selector-time per-row rejection** of rows whose key already holds a pending unit — it blocks nothing submitted later — and the bulk lock provably does not exist yet (`inst-bs-commit`: "the bulk lock takes effect on entry to `committing`"; `inst-bs-approval`: rows are "not locked while awaiting"). Since the same decision narrowed the per-row commit to the row-local set plus the touched key's window checks, a stale aggregate verdict was never re-checked at commit. Separately, the pass's stated **input** cannot decide the rules it carries: phase coverage, hybrid/per-market completeness and meter injectivity are properties of the plan's **whole** post-run row set — a selector matching only the plan's USD rows yields a frozen set that cannot evaluate per-market completeness across the plan's other markets at all. Verification built **no** concrete money-loss scenario (an interactive plan revision runs its own aggregate pass at its commit, Foundation §3.6, and the per-row unit guard still catches D-82/D-98 mismatches) — the defect is an unsupported soundness argument plus an undefined input, not a demonstrated hole.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-01** — two clauses, one instruction. (1) **Timing**: the aggregate pass runs **once per plan at the run's entry to `committing`** — inside the bulk lock, after approval — so the protection the argument appealed to actually covers the window between the pass and the per-row commits; interactive mutations committing later are covered symmetrically by their own commit-time aggregate re-run. (2) **Input**: the pass evaluates **the plan's full row set as it will stand post-commit** — the published rows with the run's successor rows substituted at the changeover instant — never the run's frozen subset alone. A plan whose aggregate pass fails still marks all its rows `failed` with the shared reason. Cost is unchanged from D-111's amortization: O(plans) aggregate evaluations per run; the per-row scope is untouched.
- **Propagated**: S12 `inst-mr-validate-scope` (rewritten tail) + `dod-mass-repricing` + §10 + integration AC (single-market selector on a multi-market plan) + NFR AC; the D-111 entry pointer.

#### D-125 [M] Unbounded read surfaces paginate — the cursor contract

- **Where**: [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §3.3 (new normative paragraph); [`design/12-operator-efficiency.md`](./design/12-operator-efficiency.md) `inst-he-read` + `inst-he-export` + §5 + `dod-history-export`; [`design/05-governance.md`](./design/05-governance.md) `inst-au-read` + §5; [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-price-history-export` + AC #40.
- **Problem** (2026-07-31d billing-domain review, M-3): no pricing document declared pagination for any collection surface — a grep over the PRD, DESIGN.md and all 12 slices returned zero hits for `paginat|cursor|$top|$skip` — while the export SLO was stated as *"p95 ≤ 5s / 100 records"*: an SLO expressed **per page with no page contract anywhere**. Affected: `GET /bss-pricing/v1/history` over ≥ 7 years of append-only rows, `GET /bss-pricing/v1/audit`, the plan and overlay lists, batch reports. Not a platform convention the gear may lean on: the sibling rating gear declares pagination in its own PRD/SEAMS, and `guidelines/` has no pagination standard.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-01** — a Foundation API convention, inherited by every slice surface: every collection, history and audit GET returns pages — `limit` (default 100, hard cap 1,000; the SLO's own unit) + an **opaque `cursor`**, `next_cursor` until exhausted. Ordering is **stable and append-consistent**: commit/append order on history/audit (a cursor walk concurrent with writes never skips or duplicates a row at or before the cursor), deterministic key order on catalog lists. Offset/`$skip` is not offered — unstable over append-only stores at the ≥ 7y retention. Exports stream the same order in bounded chunks; the p95 ≤ 5s / 100 records SLO applies per page/chunk, now with a contract defining the page.
- **Propagated**: S1 §3.3; S12 ×4 sites; S5 ×2 sites; PRD `fr-price-history-export` + AC #40.

#### D-126 [H] The first grandfathered generation is unbindable — the cohort selector resolves zero rows for the population grandfathering protects

- **Where**: [`ADR/0002`](./ADR/0002-cpt-cf-bss-pricing-adr-grandfathering-cohort-axis.md) (decision outcome + Confirmation); [`design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md`](./design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md) `inst-el-bootstrap` (new) + `dod-grandfathering` + AC ×3; [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §4.1 (`cohort` bullet).
- **Problem** (2026-08-01 review, H-1): ADR-0002 and `inst-el-generation` select the generation by the `cohort` of the subscription's **pinned price id**, adopted verbatim by Rating (`CohortGenerationSelector`; rating PRD §475/§549/§1312). A subscription predating a key's **first** cutover is pinned to a non-grandfathered row whose `cohort` is `none` — publish enforces `cohort ≠ none ⇔ existing_grandfathered`, so no generation can ever carry `none`. The selector therefore keeps **zero** rows, and rating's "no eligible price ⇒ evaluation MUST fail" applies. The class filter makes it worse: `existing_grandfathered` "includes only subscriptions activated before cutover" *promotes* the subscriber into a class whose generation selector then rejects every candidate, after the class order has already excluded the successor. The rule is self-consistent only from the **second** cutover onward; no rule defined the bootstrap, the snapshot is immutable so the cutover cannot re-pin, and rating's `CohortPin` contemplates *absence* (a torn-pin failure) but never the value `none`. Failure: at the first cutover on any key, every pre-cutover subscriber's next resolution fails closed — or, on a looser fall-through, silently resolves the `all_subscriptions` successor at the **new, higher** price, the exact outcome ADR-0002 exists to prevent. Note the asymmetry with D-04, which closed the *exit* hole (re-bind lagging `grandfatherUntil`); the entry hole was never examined.
- **Options**: (a) `cohort = none` ⇒ the generation whose `cohort` equals the pinned row's window `effectiveTo` (deterministic, snapshot+projection only, no new store); (b) first hop by `activatedAt` vs cutover, cohort-pin thereafter (a second input into a rule ADR-0002 deliberately built on one); (c) re-pin every affected subscription at the cutover (rejected — snapshots are immutable and the catalog holds no subscription state).
- **Decision**: **(a), 2026-08-01** — when the pinned price id's `cohort` is `none`, the bound generation is the one whose `cohort` equals the **pinned row's window `effectiveTo`**, which by construction of `inst-co-shorten` is the instant of the cutover that closed that row, i.e. the cutover that grandfathered the subscriber. **If no generation carries that instant** the pinned row was closed by a supersession, not a cutover: the `existing_grandfathered` class contributes **no** candidate and resolution continues down the class order — the correct "superseded, not grandfathered" outcome, and the clause that stops an empty class from failing closed. From the first renewal after the cutover the subscription re-pins to its generation and `inst-el-generation` applies unchanged. Owed adoption: **Rating** (`CohortGenerationSelector` + `CohortPin`, a SEAMS row). **Flagged for veto · joint with Rating.**
- **Propagated**: ADR-0002 (decision bullet + Confirmation test); S7 `inst-el-bootstrap` + `dod-grandfathering` + integration AC (first cutover, second cutover, supersession-closed pin); S1 §4.1.

#### D-127 [H] The cutover successor escapes the D-82/D-98/D-122 unit guard — the guard binds the key, not the mechanism

- **Where**: [`design/03-price-structure.md`](./design/03-price-structure.md) `inst-tb-supersession-units` + §5 + `dod-tier-bands` + AC; [`design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md`](./design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md) `inst-co-successor` + `dod-grandfathering` + AC; [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §4.3 (both mechanism bullets); [PRD](./PRD.md) §17.5.
- **Problem** (2026-08-01 review, H-2): D-100 made the cutover the **second** sanctioned producer of `published → superseded` on the *same* canonical scope key, and the tier counter `Q` is keyed `(subscription, meter, dimensionKey, window)` — row-blind — so it continues across a cutover changeover exactly as across a supersession. But the guard was written as *"a **usage-row supersession** MUST NOT change …"* and invoked from exactly one place, `inst-su-compose`; `algo-cutover` never cited it, the successor arrives as a client-authored "successor row ref", and nothing said it sets `supersedes_price_id`, so even a generically-registered pipeline rule had no predecessor to compare against. A cutover successor flipping `per_hour → per_day` (or `graduated → volume`, or `package_size`) applies an hours-denominated continued `Q` to day-denominated bands — the ×24 class closed four times already (D-82 supersession, D-98 kind flip, D-89 phase axis, D-113 plan change), reopened through the fifth door, on the one path that is *always material* and therefore felt safe.
- **Decision**: **2026-08-01** — the guard binds the **key**, not the mechanism: it applies to **every** usage row landing on an occupied published canonical scope key, i.e. both sanctioned producers, and **both set `supersedes_price_id` on the successor**, which is the guard's comparison referent. The rule is renamed the **succession** unit guard accordingly; `SUPERSESSION_UNIT_MISMATCH` keeps its code and gains the cutover case. The supersession-continuity fixture gains the cutover negative scenario beside the existing supersession and phase-conversion ones.
- **Propagated**: S3 `inst-tb-supersession-units` (scope clause) + §5 error text + `dod-tier-bands` + integration AC; S7 `inst-co-successor` + `dod-grandfathering` + AC; S1 §4.3 supersession and cutover bullets; PRD §17.5 normative-relationship paragraph.

#### D-128 [H] Plan retirement is a publish unit, and the projector sources the plan's current revision (`published` **or** `retired`)

- **Where**: [`design/01-foundation.md`](./design/01-foundation.md) §4.2 (new paragraph) + §4.4 + §3.7 (`pricing_plan`); [`design/11-lifecycle.md`](./design/11-lifecycle.md) `inst-rt-event` + `dod-retirement` + AC; [`design/02-plan-definition.md`](./design/02-plan-definition.md) `inst-pl-retire` + `inst-pl-supersede`; [`design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md`](./design/07-pricewindow-linkage.md) `inst-sg-surface`; [PRD](./PRD.md) `fr-plan-retirement` + §17.5 increment table.
